A truthful thought hit Tony between the ears like a nail being sunk into
soft wood by a thousand pound hammer—he did not even know this
creature's label and if this creature was even real or simply some after
effect of the peanut butter in his veins. This thought acted on Tony’s
guts like some sick juice was poured into them. The sensation acted on
them as if there was a personal earthquake gyrating in the depths of him
and shaking with a furious anger that made the blood run cold in his
veins. The sensation crept through Tony’s bowels and then tapped into
his spine creating a shiver that each individual vertebrate felt. Tony
both hated and loved the feeling because it proved his mortality and
that this was in fact not a dream.

“What are you thinking?” the red headed woman asked Tony. The words
jarred Tony’s internal introspection to bring his attention back to the
situation of this beautiful woman.

Tony’s mouth ran dry and his mind seemed to click with a fury of
responses all attempting to mask his mind raging against his social
devils.

He finally spoke, ”On your label. I was thinking about your label. I was
hoping to guess it,” Tony’s mind glanced back just a moment ago
remembering the blue arch of light they shared between their
outstretched fingers.

“A label? What should a label matter?” she asked defensively.

“Well you know mine…I only wondered about yours. Could I simply hear it
once?” he pleaded.

“I am called Personati,” the blunt tone of her voice was muted only by
the meek volume of her delicate speech traveling in the thin air between
them.

“Ahh, Neo-Lat,” a smile warmed her face upon hearing Tony’s response.

“Do you know of New-Latin?” she questioned him, “I didn’t think anybody
took the time to study anymore.”

“Yes, it’s a strange hobby of mine. I read when I am lucky enough to
find something to read but it seems I work more than I am lucky these
days. I couldn’t help but stumble upon it. It is such a beautiful facet
of speech and writing.

“Do you know its root?” She spoke with no concern paid to the fact that
they were standing in a hallway with persons walking by and in between
the two of them. She was also oblivious of the fact that this
conversation of new Latin is a conversation that only Fatherhood usually
partook in.

“Yes, with a Greek goddess…I believe by the label …Persephone,” Tony
could see her facial features relax, as if he had just given the correct
password to the doorkeeper of a speakeasy. “Goddess of the spring, I
believe—the preheat after the coldness.”

“You speak like no other I have encountered with.” Personati said as she
turned away and entered the open door of her dwelling. Her delicate
frame disappeared into the darkness for a second and then a light
illuminated the room.

“Do come in,” she was smiling as she gathered her hair behind her head
to bind it and relieve her shoulders from its shroud. Her long neck was
exposed and milky-white. Her delicate but strong frame was evident in
the visible outline of muscle under the soft flesh.

Tony stood awkwardly in the front doorway, in his mind a question was
ringing like the bell of an alarm, “What are you doing here? What’s
happening?”

“Could you close the door? Feel free to get comfortable. I'll be out in
a moment.”

Tony’s thin coat slid off his shoulders and lumped to the floor next to
the wall. He sank slowly into the gray upholstered chair-the wood
creaked under his weight. A muscle in his thigh contracted randomly,
some imbalance of minerals and chemical electricity there-in, like his
subconscious was nervously awaiting Personati’s emergence from her
bedroom.

Nervous, his eyes scanned the walls of her dwelling, they were blank
except for two brightly colored pictures adhered to the wall by sticky
clear tape at the top and bottom of the pictures. The first was of a
beach with crystal clear blue water, the white beach unscathed by any
touch, natural or unnatural, a lush green jungle dominated the
background with thick palms and tall grass. The second was of a woman in
a long robe with a yellow tinted halo circling her head. The woman’s
face was like Personati’s—the flesh of it was a pure white and there
were no lines of age or stress cutting into the youthful loveliness of
it. The woman in the picture’s robes was of an old fashion. They flowed
long and framed the angelic figure with the assistance of a beautiful
aurora of orange and yellow light. Tony stared at this picture and felt
a tear well in his eye. For some reason this man was touched by the
lovely face of the woman and could not bring himself to look away.

“Your graphicals are interesting. I’ve never seen this one of the woman
before,” Tony said while he was still staring at it.

From her bedroom Personati responded, “Oh, yes, I found them in books
that were about to be burned for fuel by the street persons. I thought
it would have been shameful to let those pictures burn. The rest of them
in the book were too filthy to salvage.”

“They’re beautiful. I wish I knew more about the woman. She seems to
have had some great importance.” Tony lamented.

“Yes, and beautiful like nothing modern I’ve found.”

Standing in the doorway between her small living room and her bedroom,
Personati leaned against the doorframe with her forearms crossed over
her abdomen. She was dressed in a light blue cotton dress that covered
her body like a thin veil just thick enough to block out the vision of
mortal eyes. Her hair was draped from her head and cascaded down her
shoulders. Tony could not see if she wore anything on her legs, she
didn’t as he caught sight of the curve of her asscheek winked from just
under the hem of her long shirt.

“They’ll be turning off the power soon. We should use the heart while we
have it,” she turned and walked into the small kitchen and set a pan of
water on the cook top of the heart.

“What’s on the menu?”

“Rations, like everybody else. Always rations boiled to taste,” Her
deadpan humor reassured him that her view on the rations was congruent
with his. “Please have a seat anywhere while it cooks.”

The mash of protein, nutrients and vitamins bubbled slowly in the pan as
she sunk a steel spoon into it and stirred.

Personati retrieved two bowls from the cupboard and readied them beside
the oven awaiting the mash to be poured into them.

Across the galley kitchen, Tony leaned against the refrigeration unit
studying Personati’s actions, as she tended to cook the food attempting
to make it palatable for consumption.

Personati divided the tiny ration into two bowls and said, “Soups on.”

They ate quietly standing in the kitchen not daring to look into each
other’s eyes but rather remaining quiet as if they were held captive in
a church partaking in a sacrament.

His eyes traced her curves and the bend of her legs.

Her eyes could not avoid the twisting bulges and veins lacing his
forearms.

As their bowls found the kitchen sink, silence took the rest of the
world away as soft tones of lovemaking began and continued into the
early hours of the next morning.

Point of No Return 

Sitting on a curb in the cool light of the early morning sun, Tony felt
the subterranean machinery begin to pull the Pedwalls back down and
relieve the streets of their segregated state. The duration counter
whirled on his wrist and reminded him that he needed to be somewhere
other than sitting atop a curb. Ghost-like figures crossed over each
other in front of Tony’s eyes. Their tattered coats clung to their
frames and their faces hovered above their chests on thin necks.

Tony pulled himself up to his feet to begin his commute still grinning
from the evening. The grind lowering the Pedwall called Tony’s thoughts
back to the present. Going to his dwelling would make him late for his
work shift. Going to work will only make the henpecking worse once he
has to go back there.

Tony’s feet felt like cement-cement covered in cement with a 1,000-pound
ball dragging behind him on a chain. His mind balanced the fallout from
each choice. Refuse work or go back to Illisa at their dwelling. The
braces on both his knees squeaked a little but the sound didn’t bother
him. The squeaks and groans of the steel wrapped around his legs were
like a rhythm that always followed Tony wherever he went. Tony had
almost forgotten about it but the vibrations of the ped wall machinery
were still registering on his sense of touch, rumbling up through the
street.

Left…right…left…right, like Tony was marching off to some far off war.
As his boots tapped the cement below them, his mind trailed to back
training before his first war.

Heat, Tony remembered the heat most of all. It soaked you. The heat
seemed to based stream and cook you all in one sitting. The breeze
offered no relief as it only pushed hot air across Tony’s dark skin.

Basic training had finished a few days before he was deployed to the
dusty sands across an ocean and beyond his comprehension of distance.
Days under deck eating hot chow and.basting in each other’s farts, he
and his fellow draftees had but a few short weeks before could not spell
the place where many of them would shortly die.

The amphibious transport reuse led a catfish as it swam toward the
shore. Lead rounds and tracer rounds pinged and glanced off the metal
fish’s armor. A Sargent barked reassuring orders to the nervous men in
the belly of the metal beast.

The rampart dropped. Soldiers and Marines poured form the gaping mouth.
Rounds, some burning hot phopospours glazed ones burnt red dashed across
the battlefield. Thousands of men poured form the mouths of the metal
mongrels leading to pockets and catches of protection from the peppering
gunfire casting itself from pillboxes and shooting blinds enjoyed by the
enemy.

Orders shouted were not ignored, they were never heard by many. Ahead of
Tony were three men. Ordered to grab the man's belt in front of him and
charge without stopping or his legs were to be shot out from under him.
Orders were orders. He felt the man behind him grip his ammo belly from
above his tailbone.

The boat’s mouth opened. Bullets buzzed by Tony’s ear. Then one chipped
his helmet. Men dropped never getting their boots wet. The hail of
bullets cut men down by twos and three pickmarkeing the lines of four
across and ten deep.

Charging ahead now, Tony tripped into the surf. Gulping down an awful
mouthful of seawater; he retched as another bullet pinged his helmet,
knocking it from his head and splashing to the water.

Another whistle swept the air just close enough to hear. Tony threw
himself into the surf as his vomit was now washing and churning around
him. He belly crawled to drier sand and found a blind where he cowered
from artillery spalling sand, water, blood, and body parts raining
around his position.

Moments passed faster than the blood pounding in his ears when the
artillery ceased. It had nearly surrounded his position but he saw a
line where it had punched through a now destroyed pillbox.

In the moment, he shot from his crouch sprinting toward the blown apart
pillbox.

His boots slapped the blasted sand. Blasting his rifle from his hip as
he charged, Tony was a spectacle of madness. And it caught fire as the
remaining soldiers spirits bounded with the show also charged ahead.

The beach was washed bloody by the end of a day Tony could not bleach
from his mindseye. That day the hero of Chesapeake Beach was born; his
legend only grew from there.

Today, Tony’s war was around the corner, waiting for him, behind him,
atop him and to each side. Tony’s war was daily against himself. The
pain wore a hole in his senses, too much and his temper sharpened to a
bitter point.

The sun was out and dancing across the sky in a slow waltz toward the
edge of the earth that no mortal ear could dream of hearing, Tony could
see the real creatures come out. The Blox had left long ago; with a
fresh corps of them now flooding into the sky above his head.

“Nobody looks into each other’s eyes,” Tony thought, “They don’t even
dare to look into mine anymore.

“Everything here doesn’t even want to see the reality of this place.” He
continued his internal rant. “Everyday the rations, power outages, the
Blox hovering overhead; this can’t be the city I was born into. This
can’t be the city my ancestors lived in. This city isn’t the same
because everyday it changes. Everyday it morphs into something even more
unrecognizable than the previous.”

The eyes of the other people on the electric tram were dim and tired,
even though it was morning and they should have been refreshed from the
night before. Their eyes inspired the feeling that Tony was the only one
left alive aboard this tram simply because he knew these dim eyed people
were not noticing the same things he witnessed.

Their faces looked like soy-meal mash-the breakfast of modern man. Their
cheeks were lifeless and drab. On the electric tram Tony stood watching
the face of a woman wiping her face in reflection one of the tram’s
glass windows. Her fingers traced lines that weren’t there five years
ago and tiredness that had not been a problem before. Grey hairs mixed
in with brown cousins along her hairline. She wore a brown skirt and
jacket - a stenographer’s aide the patch arching under a patch of three
downward point chevrons each stitched in three lines: red, white, and
blue glistened with silver thread woven into a houndstooth pattern. The
stitching created an optical illusion of the lines shifting like the
shards in a kaleidoscope.

The shake from the tram’s brake shook Tony from his stare as it slowed
to a stop. Orange webbing fluttered in the wind barring a walkway from
continuing into a reconstruction site; Tony’s jobsite.

Working Under A Microscope

The braces inflicted a dull ache into his knees. After returning to the
job site more work orders came through and rather than work Tony back
into the reconstruction site, he was sent to work on movers and
conveyances. His work orders demanded parts pulled from certain older
machines and movers. The long list had many words that Tony did not
recognize. Thumbing through the ones that he understood, he sorted the
confusing ones to the back. Maybe he’d figure something out to decode
their mystery.

The parts needed ranged from bearings to axels to whole powertrains down
to their differentials. in the sense that he would take a picture and
jumble everything up to make a puzzling mess. The mess of characters by
the end of the day—in his mind—resembled a patch of jumbled letters,
symbols, graphics and pictograms; they were all erased at the sound of a
bell marking his release from work.

Tony’s new work was suited for his mechanics, his muscle mechanics
paired well with the mental work. Washing the jumbled mess in his
brain’s fishbowl, Tony’s mind erased itself into a small space in the
back of his skull desperately attempting to disillusion his thoughts.
His mind, like that of a chemist in a lab, poured substance into
substance and studied reactions. Fascinations and errant thoughts. He
would envision the Tram suddenly jumping its track and crashing through
an apartment building then seeing the whole building tumble down.

Tony blinked his eyes and shook the vision out of his mind. The terror
and destruction, so real to his mind. Tony found these images disturbing
and did not know why he thought such awful outcomes.

Once he feared the Blox would somehow find out. They’d come to remove
him. The sting of submission spray flared a memory of its acrid sharp
stench. The worry rolled into anxiety after visions of Fatherhood
members crushed under the Tram wheels would not leave his mind’s eye.
But this never happened because the Blox did not scan for thought. They
only recorded the visual work and used Tony like a battery—a lever to
perform the tasks at hand.

Tony’s legs carried him on his daily path that all creatures inhabiting
this place were obligated to do or face the Blox and the inquisition of
the Fatherhood. Another change in work orders, Tony was to assist more
building reclaiming.

The tiles were emerald-not real emeralds, but a sick shade of green that
reminded Tony more of infection rather than a color of a jewel. They
were square tiles four inches by four inches and they covered the front
entry into the building and then led the eye to look higher as the tile
disappeared into the thick black glass and steel of the upper floors.

Tony passing by like a ghost on skates in through the out door, a Blox
hovered close by—beeping and scanning the workers as they crushed old
structures and salvaged wood, steel and brick. The workers crumbled old
structures to yield second hand materials to be patch worked into
whatever the Fatherhood deemed necessary. Metal from wires and structure
was reclaimed. Panels and other elements all that could be taken were
taken from the disused building and repurposed elsewhere. Tony had never
seen reconstruction in the city since long before the last of his wars.

The Blox floated close to every building regardless of its function. The
more secure buildings - the ones where Fatherhood members could be
found - had personal security swarms of Bloxes; always watching and
never blinking.

Tony couldn’t see his reflection in the cold stare of the Blox hovering
over the deconstruction site. Tony hated that aimless-black-digital
stare.

A cadre of Fatherhood swept past the site; one leading and the rest by
two’s trailing. Their cloaks finely embroidered with gold and silver
spun into the webbing and fabric. Symbols clade their shoulders while
the fabric rustled and floated in their motion. Black and red cloaks;
some red on the outside and others black; each with the alternate color
for its inside lining.

Tony had not noticed he now stopped his progress to and from the recycle
piles and sorting containers; however, a Blox had begun to meander
toward Tony’s now stopped frame.

Continuing to scan Tony, the Blox had recorded every inch of Tony’s
stride and body mechanizations. Bloxes scanned everything on everyone;
except the Fatherhood - cloaks shielded the sensors.

Tony began moving again toward the sorting dumpsters and began dumping
his armload. The Blox refocused its sensors and ignored Tony.

“Tony! Hammers!” The Site Foreman shouted orders while crossing the
site. “I got low numbers on the brick pile. Hammers today. Drop the
sorting for now.”

Doing as told, Tony strode to the brick pile and retrieved a
sledgehammer leaning against a chain link fence. The Bloxes hovering
nearby hanging over the back of his neck as he began breaking bricks.

Cadaver City

Strings of smoke streaked from stacks rising into the yellowing sky. The
horizon stretched into the distance and created a dark inky line where
the eyes could no longer see and the earth met the sky.

The city’s skyline scraped across a darkening sky. The skeletons of a
prior city of rang hollow in the wind. Others in a collection near its
center were the Fatherhood’s Sector. Always bright regardless of the
imposed power rationing on the remainder of the city’s brown-out prone
electrical grid.

The Ten Hour Bell rang. The workers ceased, sighed a relief then began
to wander back toward their dwellings or elsewhere.

Thudding to the ground Tony leaned it;s handle against the same chain
link fence he had found it. His hands tingle in the pits of his palms.
Stiffness would follow though his forearms. He eyed the viens traveling
just under his skin and above the muscle. The ach would take near a
whole ration of numbjuice to numb the fire in his hands; he longed for
his dwelling and the bottle he had saved.

The Tram’s windows were all sealed shut, but for one top window
whistling the gusts of wind from outside. As often not as it was the
case, the heater only worked at full speed, and could not be turned off
or down. Regardless, the tram heater churned oily scented air into the
compartment. The tram driver sweated through his uniform leaving pit
stains coursing around to the sweat lining his spine. Shifting and
wobbling with the rock of the Tram, occupants sweating each their count
of stops to relief.

There wasn’t much room to breathe. There wasn’t much room to move; there
were 10 more stops until Tony’s.

The tram dropped from speed and began to slow. A groan rolled through
the riders. The stop was another block ahead. Slapping the folding door
open and raising his hands and shifting his chest to allow the Blox to
scan his badge.

SCAN UNDER WAY. COMPLIANCE REQUESTED.

The droll burped commands started the riders to look at and shift their
bodies to show badging.

Tony rolled his eyes and drooped his head praying for the inspiration to
tolerate the boorish imposition on their commute. Resigning himself to
the search, he blinked the flicks and dark bit floating inside his eye.
Then patted his chest for his badge. It was gone.

Slapping his thighs and diving his hands into his pockets panic pulsed
through his veins as his badge was missing.

Left at the job site?

Lost? An innocent mistake?

Going right home, officer! To retrieve it and continue being the upright
citizen he sought to be.

Scanning the woman next to him now, Tony began to feel more sweat
pouring from his palms and even his legs under his coveralls. Rubbing
his hands on his legs, the Blox started to turn at Tony. then A harsh
buzz jolted Tony and a scream rent the woman’s throat beside him.

Two more long harsh, ominous sirens. Another voice cracked with a
whimper form the front of the tram.

Everyone else’s eyes cast down. Tony’s still looking up and trying
desperately to get each word in the right order when he tries to beg
forgiveness for his discovered crime.

A flash of triple lights. And a siren blasted as the Blox shot back in
quick bursts of speed to the front of the tram and flew away.

Sighs and whimpering rolled thought the riders. the driver’s hands still
shaking as he tried to grip the steering wheel to stop the shaking.

He sighed and looked up at the rear view mirror at his passengers.

“We all right. We all right, y’all.” The driver said as he slapped the
according door closed. “It’s gone.”

Desperate to occupy his racing mind with the possibility of arrest and
reservice education. It would have taken him months to find his way back
to his dwelling, even if Illisa was still there - he wept as his mind
thought of her and their first nights together. Once they held each
other coupled and locked together breathing life in and out of each
other. Nights filled with lovemaking and sheepish mornings after.

The thoughts impaled themselves against Tony’s mind. So long ago, those
memories felt buried in another timeline after the years since. Rations
while at first were bountiful had slowed and grown thinner each month
after the first year.

Then discord crept into their home. Bitterness and aloof disregard
followed the months into years while Tony and Illisa lived together and
also grew apart.

His mind pictured the memory of their first time away form the city. he
and Illisa because they were first nighters, they won a weekend retreat
while dwellings were upgraded. They found the beach enjoyable although a
beach in wintertime is less than an ideal vacation. Regardless they were
not allowed to chose when they took this vacation.

In a moment of lightheartedness, Illisa had caught a splinter in her
hand. Tony calmed and comforted her while he coaxed the splinter out by
sucking her finger. Taken aback, Illisa marveled at the man sucking her
finger, but also showing warm emotions to her. She relented., They made
love the whole weekend. Never bothered to visit the beach.

Jutting and bouncing along the rail the tram jostled her riders.

The years since those warmer days had cooled their relationship. And
once the perks of the deal ran low and subpar, the relationship grew
cold and distant.

Curving onto a diagonal street the tram slammed the riders to one side.
Tony’s knees raked the steel frame of its wooden seats. The strike of
pain seared across Tony’s senses.

Growling curses, he looked up and thought he missed his block.
Grimacing, he tugged on the stop request cord and climbed his way to
limp off the tram.

The rush of blood sent endorphins through his now dull roaring knee.
Soothing it, he staring them his back and looked upward to see a street
sign he had not intended to see. Thinking he was nearer his dwellling’s
HomeBlock he was nearly eight blocks away.

Then after cursing himself for the error he drained his head up and down
the street praying to see another tram. He did not, rather he caught
sight of a fiery mane of red hair. His mind whiffed the sweet scent of
her skin. Then the clash of his skin against hers and the grip and pull
of their lovemaking a few nights before.

Under a flickering street lamp, Tony felt the Pedwalls grow under foot
as Personati smiled and greeted him warmly as she reached the stoop of
her brownstone apartment building.

 

Toiling Under The Fatherhood

Stiff and achy at his wrist and elbows, Tony also felt tired and weak,
but this was not unusual from everyday he worked. His knees
simultaneously popped under the support of his braces, as he stood
upright. The sensation of ligaments and cartilage releasing the tension
of a day full of physical activity sent a surge of heated pain
throughout his legs and then a warm numbness cooled the nerve endings
and pressure points.

Tony stepped slowly maneuvering the rigid stiffness out of his joints
and lower back. The lock to his dwelling required a strong turn of the
wrist to open it—there was a buildup of rust and grime in the inner
mechanisms. The hinges were subject to the same neglect and they creaked
when the door was pushed open, announcing Tony’s entrance into his
dwelling like a chorus of dented trumpets.

“Where did you sleep last night?” Illisa stood hip slacked, arms
crossed.

Her face was pale with cheeks that sunk into themselves. Around her eyes
there lay thick black circles like deep engravings on a stone gargoyle.
In her eyes one could see thin veins that formed a red web over the
whiteness of them. She was holding, in her left hand, a bottle of clear
liquid with a paper label on the bottle half peeled off by moisture
residue. In her right hand a yellowed cigarette was smoldering with an
inch long ash extending out of the end of it. She was wearing a dark
gray dress with stains on the abdomen and chest.

“Nice to see you, too,” Tony muttered under his breath as he turned to
close the door.

“What did you say? Mumble-mouth!”

Illisa and Tony had been living here for the past three years. Prior to
and after Tony’s last war, The Fatherhood passed a directive requiring a
social lottery to be held. All of age and not actively coupled had their
identification numbers paired and entered into the lottery machine and
the winners of it were paired together by some matrix of compatibility
and assigned housing. The “lottery” served two purposes; one to relieve
the housing demands and secondly to address the population decline in
only the able-bodied workers section of society.

In Tony’s mind he could not see how they were to solve any of the
housing demands by directing persons to commit relations with complete
strangers in the cramped spaces of old dwellings. This realization was
reaffirmed every time Tony heard the squeak of the rusty door hinges
accompanied by Illisa’s tired and often inhospitable reception.

“I got caught on Naim last night by the Pedwalls. I had to sleep under
refuse boxes to avoid the Blox.”

“Why were you on Naim. You don’t have any business over there,” she took
a hard pull of her bottle of liquid as ash fell from the tip of her
yellow cigarette. “That's off the Tram’s route. You fucking cheater!
Lying bastard!”

Tony turned away from her inquisition and pulled his coat from his
shoulders and hung it on a hook that stabbed into the wall next to the
door.

As her accusations continued, Tony trudged toward the kitchen's
refrigeration unit. His hands now ached, the numbness had long since
fuzzed itself away with the dull ache he was now feeling replacing it.

A static ringing entered his ears. Illisa’s questions left unanswered
opened the cooler to retrieve his ration of numbjuice.

Illisa stopped and took a swig from the bottle in her hand.

Tony gripped the bottle's neck and wrenched the cap from its top with
his other hand. Taking a swig, the alcohol sting was weak and tasted
more of water than of the numbing sensation the label stated.

“Why does this taste like water?” he asked without turning around.

“How..how should I…” Her nagging ceased. “How should I know why your
Numb-Juice tastes strange. I don’t touch your stuff. How dare you accuse
me…”

He didn’t stop to listen. Instead, he returned the bottle and stepped
aside of her out of the kitchen and toward the bedroom throughout the
common area. Her stare continued persecuting him for his absence and
obliviousness.

Now in the bedroom, Tony laid down on the bed with his hands still
pulsing with a dull ache and a static sound in his ears. He stared
directly above him at the ceiling and noticed a crack in it. The crack
itself had not been there the first time he had laid down in that bed,
it had grown over time along with various other cracks in the walls of
the dwelling.

Rolling to his left side, he noticed another crack running diagonally up
the wall reaching and spreading in a wild pattern with stucco chips
powered on the floor below. Twisting his shoulders and rolling over he
decided to bury his head instead of stare at everything wrong with his
world. Tony buried his face in a thin pillow and pinched his eyes closed
to wallow in the darkness in an attempt to bring about some semblance of
sleep and relaxation.

He had just relaxed enough to hear a pot crash to the floor from the
kitchen in the dwelling next to his and a baby’s cry rent the air. His
eyes opened like twin throttles in the carburetor of an engine. He
pulled himself up from his uncomfortable dozing to see that his boots
were still on his feet and his braces were still choking his knees.

The pain, covering his body, had in some sense eased. With a little deep
breathing as he sat at the edge of his bed he could feel the tension
exit his shoulders and the numbness after taste of the pain wash slowly
over him. Tony imagined a ball rounding in his mouth; all the painful
impulses emanating from his ears, shoulders and hands. The mound rolled
like a bitter gumdrop in his jaw. And with a natural reflex of his
digestive system he swallowed hard and the sensation left him.

He stood up and stepped out of his bedroom to see if Illisa was waiting
for him to allow her to continue her interrogation. She was sitting in a
thickly stuffed chair, which allowed her to sink into it slightly. Her
bottle of Numb-Juice was still in her left hand only it was now almost
empty and her cigarette was smoldering on the nub of the filter.

She stared at him with glazed eyes as he reached for his coat hanging on
the hook, which was stabbed into the wall.

“Illisa,” Tony spoke after standing over her in an uneasy silence, ”I’m
leaving,” he pulled his coat over his broad shoulders, “You don’t need
me here anymore than you want me here. If you’re going to report me,
then please wait a day or until next week’s first day to do so. Gimme a
few days head start if you could.”

“Pfft,” Illissa drunkenly scoffed. “Going to Naim Street?” She sat in
her chair, legs splayed before her. Her short stained nightgown half
ruffled up near her crotch. Tony’s eyes cast by the furry darkness just
under her gown. Her small breast looked like two pyramids striking at
her off-white and stained night gown. Her chest rising and lowering as
she stumble-stood and growled at him.

“Go on. Find that trashbox you slept in last night”

“I’m gone.” tony locked eyes with Illisa. “I don’t give a damn what you
do.”

For a moment there could have been a chance this course reversed itself.
There was a moment where Illisa reached for Tony, and he for her. A
moment where they fell into each other and fought each other’s demons
while releasing their own grappling each other naked and toward the
bedroom. This was not the case.

“You ain't got the gut to do nothing! You’ll be back.” She had retrieved
a half-smoked cigarette from the ashtray on the kitchen table and
pointed it at him punucating her rant. “Every time you run away you come
back. You’re just stupid little man! You’ll be back.”

She stopped, blinked, and suddenly felt the cigarette buring her
fingers. Exclaiming and faking a fall she slipped into the chair. her
round backside bouncing her out of the chair and onto the floor. Her
nightgown had ridden up past her hips now and her womaness was exposed.
She hissed at him and slapped the floor.

“You’ll never get back in this juicy pussy! Naw, big boy, you ain’t got
no claim to this fat box! Go on! Run off to the next…nnnn..nn next
whore!”

“You’re drunk-I’m leaving,” his words animated her body to attempt to
get up but she was too weak from the Numb-Juice to give much effort.
Tony crossed the room to leave.

“Good!” she threw her now empty bottle against the wall of the dwelling.
The thin glass crashed against it and rained down onto the hard bare
floor. “Go find you a whore, but don’t expect to come back here or have
a place to have the Blox bring you back too!”

A moment. The door just slammed shut. Illisa was on one side; Tony the
other. She sobbed and snorted back tears and phlegm. She trailed back to
the bedroom resigning from the fight after she knew he wasn’t coming
back this time.

Out on the street. Tony was out and walking down toward an alleyway that
lead to a twist of alleys lead him to a gated tunnel left ajar.

He stood there for a moment

“You’re a fool! You’re going to fail and everything will crumble around
you and then you yourself. You’re a blind fool! Go back to her and take
your ration in this life.” His voice plied him with a loathsome
solution.

“The semblance of choice is the figment of freedom.”

His footsteps tapped the concrete in the tunnel and then grew wetter as
the light left the darkness. Ahead a low yellow glow grew. He pushed his
hands deep into his coat pockets. The quiet squeaking of the knee braces
began to sing their narration for Tony’s walk. He saw a few empty
numbjuice bottles glistening in the lamplight. Papersm, trash, and
debris littered underfoot.

Coming to an abrupt and strange dead end, Tony puzzled at the wall that
should not be where it was. In the low light he counted steps and
pointed his thoughts toward his path.

Sudden shaking. Dust and dirt shuddered from the bricks lining the
tunnel over Tony’s head. Terrified of a cave in, he waited for the
ceiling to drop in on him and end his experience.

The wall in front of him began to shift upwards. The Pedwalls had just
begun to rise.

Old Friends and New Peanut Butter

Days later, on the surface, Tony sat at a small table and a fresh print
off of an announcement was pinned under a small cup of
thin-brown-lukewarm liquid that was called “coffee” but only by the menu
hung on the wall.

Called a distrocenter, the Fatherhood relabeled them after the
cultural-evolution and promised all citizens a cup a day and news
printed for their intelligence. A citizen could get the coffee and an
announcement or attempt to bribe the counter worker for a small
allotment of Numb-Juice.

Tony remembered when money transferred between hands instead of ID cards
being shown like badges of shame. After a cultural evolution not too
long ago the ID card surfaced. The Blox appeared soon afterwards to
enforce the use of the ID cards. Soon the money became scarcer. Now
there was no money. Only the ID cards and the job you had to work at to
keep your ID card valid.

The process was simple; wait in line-that curved around the corner and
down the block; hand your ration/citizen/ID card to the worker standing
at the counter; receive your small cup of “coffee” and yellowed copy of
the current announcement. They would allow you to sit at one of the
small tables for a time, but when the fifteen duration bell rang you
were required to stand up and move to another table-which may or may not
have had a chair at it for you to rest in. A Blox hovered in the corner
enforcing these rules and ignoring the black market deals concerning the
numbjuice.

The bell rang and like trained mice everyone stood up. Tony resolved to
lean against a wall instead of fighting for a seat. Tony watched this
scene as he leaned against the dingy wall.

The persons followed the bell prompt then the amusing dance of the
people as they switched tables for chairs. Then he saw a woman walk by a
large yellowing window at the front of the distro-center. Her thin perky
body was encased by a yellow and pink dress covering her like a fresh
case of herpes.

The colors of the dress marked her as property of the Fatherhood and she
flaunted her body with the swing of her hips and the subtle bounce of
her small breasts. The poor gray workers trapped in the distro-center
stared longingly at the display of woman hood passing before their eyes
on the other side of the yellowing glass.

Tony tossed his small empty cup into the recycling bin, folded a copy of
today’s announcement underarm, and stepped outside.

Perched atop a curb, it was chipped and marred by years of abuse and
neglect; Tony was staring into the bleak muddy color of the wastewater
as it slowly drained into the darkness of the sewer. A pair of boot
covered feet appeared next to Tony’s. His eyes traced the figure of a
portly man wearing a thick black coat with his hands pushed into the
front pockets of his pants.

“Tony!” The voice seemed familiar as Tony’s eyes locked onto the warm
brown tint of Willie’s eyes staring back from the confines of a pudgy
face. “Good to see you again, man!”

Willy brushed his thickly sheathed arm against Tony’s.

“Willy…Hey man. Yeah, Willie,” Tony couldn’t exactly place the sensation
welling up inside of him. A sick quivering-bubbling up feeling in Tony’s
guts took to motion. Tony’s upper lip boiled with beads of sweat and
awkward coolness.

“Strange things afloat with the wind today. I see all these fools
trudging through each inch of their lives with the yoke of these black
boxes hovering above their heads. But, you know what brother?” Willie
nudged Tony’s arm, “I’m feeling pretty cool right now with all the
things I got going and cooking in my world these days. I don’t mind what
they think. Not anymore. Ever since I got that real butter. Ohh, I can’t
get enough of it. You know the reports they run on us when the
evolutions were in full effect.”

The wind whistled into Tony’s left ear like a bird in a tree. The sharp
notes of the harsh cold pressure were punishing the skin of his inner
ear. Willie was speaking into Tony’s right ear.

Willie continued speaking: “They said I was one of those people who
drink and eat like they have nothing else to do. They said I would be
great at managing the supplies to the city. You know catching the barges
and dividing them for the equal distribution to the people.

They said I was a leader, somebody to lead the poor masses. I just
didn’t have the stuff, genetically for the bright colors.”

Tony rolled his eyes and pushed his hands deeper in his empty pockets.

“Listen man you got some time; wanna have some fun-some of that illegal
stuff?” Willie’s lips and thick black mustache framed his off white
teeth.

“Yeah I got a little,” Tony nervously replied.

“Icy…Real icy man. Come on, you gotta see something.”

Willie’s weight shifted strangely beneath the thick coat as he walked in
front of Tony. His thick legs stamped down out of the shroud of the coat
like twin inverted pistons moving a strange inverted engine. Willie led
Tony down an alleyway past refuse bags and refuse bins. His hands pulled
back full refuse bags and tossed them aside like large heads of cabbage.
Slowly his actions revealed a small door with a tiny handle only big
enough to turn with two fingers. The door was rose colored and inched
into the corner of two walls.

Willie then turned back to face Tony, a sober look embraced his face,
and said, “Let’s put this baby in the oven.”

Willie then pressed two fingers against the small handle of the rose
colored door. His chubby digits pulled the door open and a gust of warm
air pushed out of the opening across Tony’s face. Willie squeezed into
the opening and with a hand extending after his frame motioned for Tony
to follow him into the darkness.

“Warm down here ain’t it,” Willie spouted as he stripped the thick heavy
coat from off his rotund body. “Go ahead, take off ya’ coat. We got some
walking to do down here in this wonderful heat.”

Tony’s coat slipped off his shoulders and dropped to a heap next to
Willie’s. There was a warm sweet vinegar-powder smell to this place.
Tony could taste his nostrils as he followed his portly friend down a
tunnel towards a dim yellow light ahead of them.

“Have you ever been down here before, buddy?” Willie questioned.

“No. Didn’t know a place like this existed. Is this where they make the
PB?”

“Naw, but I get it from down here. Can’t believe you've never been down
here?” A laugh bellowed out of Willie’s mouth, “Caught me a virgin…a
newbie…an A-MA-TUER!” he over pronounced the last depiction on purpose.

Tony’s feet moistened as a thin stream of water streamed under his heavy
steps in the bottom of this strange new place.

“A verbal riot,” Tony thought to himself, “A verbal riot where there is
no cohesion with any hope of comprehension. All their conversations
impede on the hearing senses-everything so ill focused and random, like
some riot fueled by the substance of illegal essence all rolling on the
tongues and in the bellies of the participants. A wild discourse is
occurring around me.”

The room was bathed in a strange warm yellow glow from a single bulb
descending out of the ceiling. The bulb’s glow cast strange shadows
behind the figures of the people in the room; many ghostlike shapes
dance on the walls behind them. Two empty jars sat at the center drain
of this circular room.

“Nothing… Nothing but the best masturbatory butt grammar for the kings
of this existence,” Willie’s words glanced off the ears of all those who
heard him and none truly understood his statement. The people in this
room were all subject to their own conversations and their own intent
thoughts and descriptions. Their social discourse sounded harshly in
echoes against the cold concrete walls.

This chamber was a pit, a den somewhere lost below the surface that
seemed so far removed from the world above.

Then a rumbling began; the Pedwalls were rising by actions of the
machinery in the underground.

A fear sounded in between Tony’s ears, “I’m not going to remember this
place until I’m gone.” Tony studied the faces of these people in some
vain attempt to remember the freest moment of his life he had ever felt.
A steel tumbler was passed to Tony and the cool liquid sloshed inside as
Tony raised it to his mouth. The water, which tasted of the purest kind,
washed down his throat and its coolness flooded his abdomen like a good
bath floods the dirt from off ones soul. As the water flooded into him,
Tony saw a face staring back at him from the bottom of his cup. Tony
grinned as he lowered the cup from his lips and began to relax his frame
in the midst of the new people in the chamber.

Every culture, however subterranean, confusing or alien, still retains
core elements of the human influence and condition that are inherent in
the idea of society. Order out of chaos and man’s accent from the state
of nature is subject to any amount of his freedom being impeded upon and
therefore begins the backslide into the decline of conditions by way of
choice. Tony couldn’t help but think those words while sitting in the
midst of these lost souls.

Willy’s dark black hair glistened under the yellow glow of the bulb. His
eyes scanned the room and a smile spread across his face under the thick
black mustache. If any of the poor souls in that underground could have
seen behind Willie’s eyes they would have seen the thirst for dominance
and not brotherly love. The grin, focused more on himself than whom he
was smiling at, stained Willie’s face as he watched Tony examine this
strange new place with virgin eyes.

Tony was like a child here in this new place—a candy store of new faces
and surroundings.

The walls of the chamber were ribbed with brick and mortar. The crates
they sat on were of wood and bound by wire and nail. A thin trickle of
water ran through the middle of the chamber dividing the sides of it.
Willie sat upon a large crate; it strained to hold his heavyweight. Tony
was facing Willie as if their seating was the face of a clock-Tony was
six and Willie was twelve. Began to introduce Tony to all people in the
chamber, but Tony was not concerned with labels at the first
introduction, he believed that if their labels were important then he
would learn them by association. Tony’s eyes, instead, gazed over the
whole of the chamber admiring the domed ceiling ribbed with ancient
brick. The redness of the bricks themselves reminded Tony of an open
wound.

There were two entrances to this chamber. One Tony knew would lead him
back to the small rose door and the aboveground. The second, which was
at the opposite side of the chamber, led to a darkened tunnel and
stretched around a corner.

“Tony!” Willie sounded, “Put whatever money you got down for the buy.”

All their eyes were focused on him. Willie was standing and his seat was
placed in the center of the chamber over the drain with a small pile of
gold, money, and shiny things were placed upon it, like an altar.

“Oh, Oh yes… uh …yeah,” Tony nervously searched his pockets for his
valuables as all sets of eyes focused solely on his ability to produce
some amount of money to collectively feed each of their individual
cravings.

Tony pulled a small pouch filled with metals from his hip pocket, Tony
meekly reached forward and dropped the wad onto the small pile of
valuables.

“Okay,” Willie said as soon as the pouch hit the pile and reached
forward, wiping the money into a small sack, “Tony and I are going for
it. The rest of you stay here.”

As Willie stood back upright his head tapped against the yellow bulb
sending it swinging like a noose in the breeze. Willie’s eyes locked
onto Tony’s waiting gaze and gestured to the second entrance opposite of
the first one. As they left Tony’s eyes glanced back at the light bulb
swing in the chamber. The swinging motion of the light gave the faces of
the people in the chamber a still animation where the details of their
faces were exposed with the strained angles of light hitting them.
Shadows danced like ghosts on the walls. Their conversations remained
paused and nobody stood up to steady the swinging yellow light bulb.

The tunnel got darker with every step the two of them took towards a
blue speck gleaming at the two of them from the darkness. Tony’s steps
seemed to get deeper into something semi-soft. As they trudged along the
tunnel got a bit colder and the two of them passed branch after branch
of tunnels each gusting various strengths of drafts of air. The blue
glow grew into a soft glaze against the slicked walls of the tunnel.

Willy dared to break the silence, “Not what you expected huh, man?” the
question trailed behind him as he pressed ahead into the blue glazed
darkness.

“Never been below like this, don’t know what to expect,” Tony replied.

“Good,” Willie’s response was short and calculated-calculated to leave
Tony uneasy and off balance as if placed on stilts during a windstorm.

“Willy, where do these tunnels lead?”

“Nowhere and everywhere,” his puzzling retort wafted over his shoulders,
“You can get anywhere in the city you just have to want to walk through
it.”

“Walk through what?” Tony questioned.

“Look down and feel what is sludging under your feet.”

Sludge is too kind of a word. The sick runoff of every backside of every
creature that was ever hit with any bit of filth mixed with their own
and then tossed down the drain, that is what Willie referred too. If it
wasn’t for the sweet scent of peanut butter permeating through the
tunnel Tony might have added more to the sludge they were mutually
trudging through.

Tony’s mind filled with the sweet idea of the peanut butter, the sweet
opiate-like gooeyness, that was worth every step through the flushings
of society, as they stepped out of the tunnel and he saw jars and jars
stacked on shelves. Golden heaven in a jar is all that Tony’s eyes could
see.

“Willy!” snapped a cat-like woman from behind the makeshift counter.

“Lucinda, Darlin’! How are you doing today? Business Good?”

“Better now that one of my best toilers has come a shoppin’. Who’s the
new face?”

“This be Tony. A fresh mouth that loves the golden stuff.” They were now
standing side by side-Tony and Willie in front of the counter.

Lucinda, with a cat-quickness, reached out and grabbed Tony by the
collar of his shirt and pulled close to her chest.

“Hmmm,” she sniffed at his scalp and traced the muscles of his shoulders
under his thin shirt, “Fresh from atop, huh?” Her graceful hand traced
up from his collarbone and ended with her palm against his cheek. She
clasped Tony’s cheeks in both her paw-like hands and looked most
uncomfortably into his eyes.

A smile inched across her face and she spoke, “I like you… Smell like a
man should smell.” She then quickly licked the tip of his nose and
released his face from her grasp pushing him away like he was a
firecracker about to explode-perhaps he could have, “So how many jars
today, Willie.”

“Two golden, three of the normal.” Willie’s eyes were aimed like guns at
Tony’s bewildered face.

“Two golds… three smiths,” Lucinda mimicked the request and slid a total
of five jars across the counter top to Willie. She then reached out and
grabbed Tony’s chin to steady it for a surprise. “Try this sweet smell!”
She said as a claw full of white peanut butter plunged into Tony’s open
mouth.

The taste was like everything one could dream, taste and smell. It was
nothing like peanut butter and yet everything one would enjoy about
peanut butter. He could feel the intoxication invading his body even
without swallowing it. The buttery concoction felt like a thousand
pixies floating in his mouth and dancing in some strange chaotic
pattern. Tony’s mouth watered over the claw full of pure pleasure he had
just been force-fed.

“Something to keep you showin’ back up way down here, Sugar,” her voice
was sultry, sweet and creamy just like the intoxicating substance she
pedaled.

Willy gathered up four jars and motioned for Tony to get the last.
Willie then turned to exit the room and Tony followed behind him. As
Willie disappeared into the darkened tunnel Tony looked back to see
Lucinda softly kneading a pile of valuables left atop the countertop.

“See you soon, Sugar,” her words nudged at Tony’s shoulder and back like
a cat saying hello to their owner in the morning.

The sweet claw full of elixir was slowly dissolving in Tony’s mouth.

“Don’t get too comfortable with all that, Tony. She did the same with
me,” Willie’s lie wafted over his shoulders like a bad smell.

They walked back in the blue glazed tunnel but Willie paused half way
back at one to the branches of the tunnel.

“Hmmm…let’s try this one,” Willie turned to his left and detoured from
the path that would have directly led them back to the chamber. Willie
stepped down into a gray tinted small closet of a room and turned to
Tony.

“All right, get out that jar you picked up,” Tony complied with Willie’s
command. “Let’s open her up and sample.”

Tony spun the top from off the jar as Willie produced two silver spoons.

“Go ahead, man,” Tony offered the first spoonful to Willie, “Start it
off.”

Willy started with a reserved dose of peanut butter on the silvery
spoon. Tony followed with a more liberal amount.

“Be careful! You don’t wanna get too much too soon,” Willie warned.

The both of them spooned dose after dose of the sweet gooeyness into
their gaping jaws.

Choking down a spoonful, Willie broke the silence, “We’ll tell them
prices have gone up.”

“Why tell them at all?” Tony sharply replied, “By the looks of them I
don’t think we got a bunch of penny pinchers in there. We could have a
small army if we wanted. I know I ain’t got nothing above to go back
too. I was running away when you found me.”

Willy paused and lowered the spoon from his lips, “Well, we’ll need a
symbol for all of this. If we’re gonna have ourselves a regular
underground existence,” Willie had traces of peanut butter in the
creases of his off-white teeth.

Tony started, “How about a spoon…”

“In a fist,” Willie finished.

They nodded their approvals and continued the attack on the now half
empty jar of peanut butter. They repeated their actions of spoonful
after spoonful into their open mouths soon exhausting the contents of
the jar.

“Let’s go feed the masses,” Tony said and stood up and exited the small
room. Willie nodded and followed behind Tony after tossing the jar into
a dark corner of the small room.

The Underground Takes Root

Personati slept as a duration counter whirled. She was assigned to work
today, but she remained asleep. The morning’s sun shined in through the
small window at an acute angle shinning onto the floor. A thin pillow
leaned against the bed; bed sheets and a tattered blanket twisted
themselves into a heap that lay diagonal across the bed. Out from under
the heap of sheets a white hand reached out and pulled them back to
reveal a redheaded beauty lying on her back…naked.

She stirred softly in response to the exposure of light. She slowly sat
up and stretched her arms out above her head as if she was a slow motion
footage of a flower rising out of dirt. The duration counter on her
bedside table whirled as she threw the covers off her body and too the
floor. Her naked body then rolled out of bed and she stood in front of
the open window where the sun made her body glow with a radiant aurora.

The time displayed on the face of the duration counter read
nine-thirty-two. Right now Personati would be performing some task as
mind numbing as mind numbing could be, but today she had overslept…on
purpose.

“I’m not going back,” she thought to herself, ”Why should I? If I go
back I’ll not receive any punishment for my absence. Where is the
punishment? The Blox will come for me when they feel I have had enough
of my fun. I can almost hear their buzzing at the door. I can hear their
metallic prongs stab into the lock on the door and I can see them coming
through the door. They’ll inject me with some drug to make me sleep and
remove me. They’ll take me to reeducation. Weeks perhaps months will
pass and then I’ll find myself at work trudging through the minutia of
papers and s. Reading a thousand pictograms and graphics and cataloging
the dead records of this crumbling city. Why… Why should I allow it to
bother me any more…if they want me they will come and find me. I’m not
going back.”

She turned from the window and began to dance about the room. Feeling
more free than she had ever felt before she could not think of anything
but the single spark of blue light she had felt the night before with a
man she did not know then but knew more of now. He had kissed her
goodbye in the early morning and whispered in audible sweet nothings. He
was gone but she knew he would soon return. She knew she could not stay
here and trust that the Blox would not come for her.

“I’ll look for Tony on the street. I can hide in the alleyways if the
Blox come.”

She began to dress her body with her cleanest clothes. In a small bag
she placed a three days worth of rations. She pulled the two pictures
from off the wall, folded them gently and placed them in her small bag.
With her hand on the doorknob of her front door she remembered something
was missing. She returned to her bedroom and reached under the bed to
retrieve a box eight inches by five inches and three inches deep. She
kissed the lid of the box and pulled from it a dark beaded necklace that
had an intersecting symbol tethered to it and placed it around her neck.
She locked the door from the outside and casually walked down to the
street level.

###

The chamber was dim; Willie had adjusted the glow of the yellow bulb so
the euphoric sensation of the peanut butter would ease the lot of them
into sleep. Tony was stirring in a semi-sleep; he was dreaming again
about a woman he knew but didn’t know. His eyes opened slowly with a
dream still on the lips of his mind. He knew he had to go find her if
only to see her once more he must find her.

Tony’s back was stiff from lying at an angle against the bricks of the
chambers walls. Tony’s duration counter told him that he had only three
hours before the Pedwalls come up. Tony had to get to Naim Street before
the walls came up. But more importantly he had to find Personati.

Tony had one foot in the tunnel leading back to the rose colored door
when a voice sounded over his shoulder.

“Where you going?” Willie’s voice was cool and rigid.

“Back up top. I gotta find somebody and get the rest of my stuff.”

“You can’t. The Pedwalls will get you caught by the Blox.”

“Too bad, I’m going up top. I’ll be back later.”

“Tony, we can’t have people coming in and out of here like it was a tram
station. We gotta look out for the Blox. We can’t let them find this
place.”

“I gotta take the chance.” Tony turned and stepped into the darkness.

Left in the dim glow of the yellow bulb, Willie steamed at Tony’s
insubordination. Inside his mind like a strange duration
counter–calculator a devious plot was hatching on how one day nobody
would ever cross his judgment.

The air brushed across Tony’s face like a headache as the rose colored
door snapped shut behind him. Tony stepped out of the alleyway to see
the street spotted with people and moving in patches like blood clots in
a vein.

Tony’s steps carried him slowly at first, but hen his mind began to
command them to move more swiftly. Soon he was running it felt strange
to him because Tony had never run before. He could feel the blood in his
veins race all over his body. The sensation was beautiful to him; he
could not place exactly why it pleased him but he knew that performing a
forbidden act was invigorating. Naim Street was eight blocks away.

The persons on the street stared at the sight of this illegal act. He
streaked by them and his lungs burned with the sensation of being alive.
The blood in his veins pulsed and raced with the gyrations of his
muscles and the swing of his arms. Their faces blurred in the
perspective of Tony’s eyes and he focused keenly on the concrete path
ahead. A sensation of pain stabbed into Tony’s side and his heart seemed
as though it were to burst from the abuse of running, but Tony refused
to stop running. His thoughts centered on the fact that this sensation
was life stabbing him into his side and making his heart cry out form
mercy. Tony could not stop his running; he felt that even if a wall to
be erected in front to o him, he would not slow or change his course-he
would simply run through it.

Sweat beaded on the surface of his skin and with the awesome spectacle
of what can only be described as a maddening beast racing wildly toward
the future. The people witnessing this sight were awestruck and scared.
The sight of his massive arms swinging tandem and the powerful bursts of
air shooting in and out of his lungs seemed to be both beautiful and
frightening to them. This was forbidden by directive of the Fatherhood .
They said our society had advanced beyond the need of such frivolous
activity such as running. The directive said it was wasteful and
selfish, ignorant of the feelings of those that could not run.

“If everyone can not run, then no shall run instead,” that is what their
directive read. Those words were burning now like the air in Tony’s
lungs. He was now only two blocks away from Naim Street. His legs ached
with the rush of blood in them and there was a pinching sensation in
between his shoulder blades, but Tony could not slow his pace because he
saw a ped wall begin to rise out of the ground. The walls were rising
early. Perhaps the Blox had seen him running, perhaps someone had
alerted them to his reckless selfish rule breaking.

“I can not stop now that I am so close. Faster! Faster!” Tony’s inner
monologue sang like the mob crashing the palace gates in his head.

The Pedwall rose to a foot out of the ground and inching higher. Tony’s
stride strained to quicken. He was only fifty feet away from it and the
ped wall continued to gain in height. Every step Tony’s momentum carried
him was another amount of wall that rose out of the earth. His arms
thrashed wildly and his legs screamed with the weight and momentum
building against them—still the wall was imposing itself against his
path. Twenty more feet-the wall was already at the height of Tony’s
knee. The power pulsing through Tony’s vein was like gristle burning in
a red hot skillet-the thought entered Tony’s mind that he should run
through the wall. Then reality struck him between his eyes and Tony’s
jumped with all of his strength he jumped into he air. His body was like
a projectile erupting out of a cannon Tony’s chest arms and upper half
cleared the wall, but the walls accent to the sky did not slow and his
legs were punched at the knee by the cold concrete of the Pedwalls top
sending his large airborne frame tumbling heels-over-head.

Tony lay in a heap in the middle of the street. His body was covered in
sweat and his chest was heaving his deep breaths. Tony rolled onto his
back and a loud chuckle escaped his mouth. He was laughing at the
sensation that was coursing through him. It felt like a lighting bolt
had just possessed his body. At this moment, without the aid of peanut
butter or Numb-Juice, Tony felt more alive than he ever felt before.
Indeed he was alive-the air rushing in and out of his lungs-the blood
rushing in his veins and muscles-the laughter bouncing off the walls
cold impotent surface was all evidence proving to his mind he was.

Tony sat up with a jerk of his arms upwards. His right knee sang with a
loud pinch. His brace had been bent in the altercation with the wall.
Tony’s hands twisted off the clamps and bent back the inferior metal
away from his knee. The brace dropped to the ground as Tony rolled onto
the palms of his hands and sprang to his feet.

The weight felt strange on his knee now that he was freed from the
confines of the brace. He stamped down on his right foot and laughed a
great belly laugh:

“Ha! A-ha-ha-ha! And there sits all the lie mother state told me.”

He crouched down and retrieved the tangled metal brace with his right
hand and turned it over in his hands examining the evidence of the lie
he had be told all his life. It was a directive that put him in those
braces. They said his back was weak and his knees needed support, also
his knees were formed poorly and that he needed these braces to walk.
The metal glistened with scratches and bends as Tony rubbed the tips of
his fingers over the remains of the brace.

“Tony!” a familiar voice rang out to him.

He raised his head to see Personati standing across the street. She was
staring in awe at the sequence of events she had just seen.

“Did you see? I beat the wall!” Tony boastfully shouted to her. She
walked slowly to him not daring to repeat his actions for fear of the
Blox swooping down for the both of them.

“I saw it…but…why did you do that…you could have been killed,” her voice
was nervous.

“I needed to find you.” He said as he dropped the remains of the knee
brace to the ground and reached to embrace her. “I had to find you.”

They embraced briefly and he dared to kiss her on the lips.

It was a slow tentative kiss daring to ignore past cultural-declaration
set down by power mad brains in ignorant hackneyed response to idiot
situations.

“We can’t stay out here. They might come for you.” She whispered to him.

“Sure they might come, but wouldn’t that imply some amount of
efficiency?” he grinned as he looked into her beautiful green eyes. She
smiled and turned to lead him by the hand up to her dwelling.

In her mind she thought, “What a man this man could be? He has done so
much just to find me. Those motions of speed he performed; his flight of
defiance over the Pedwalls and the beautiful arch of his body in the
air. Those strange sounds he makes his voice like a thunderclap coming
from his chest. Even if the Blox come I would believe he would simply
smash them with a fist.”

An Underground Refuge

A warm sensation snuggled against his side has the morning sun shined
warmly on his eyelids. He could smell the scent of her hair—a sweet
natural womanly scent. Under the curls of her hair lay her milky white
face. Her lips, red by virtue of warm blood in her veins, quivered with
a dream in her mind fueling them. She burrowed against his warmth.. Her
hand reached out too lay across his chest. The tip of her nose,
resembling the angular lines a sculptor might employ to give life to
this innate creation, rested suspended from her creamy white face—air
pulsing in and out through the nostrils. He stared at the face of this
creature-the smooth line of her jaw, which ran from the underside of her
ear to the curve of her chin. Her eyebrows arched over her closed almond
shaped eye and added grace to her peaceful face. A light strawberry curl
dropped from atop her head to give an impromptu aspect to this beautiful
sight.

“Good morn’ to you,” her soft words drifted like a kiss to Tony’s ears.

“Good morn, Miss Personati.”

Her right hand reached up to touch his cheek and drew him closer still.

“I never want to leave you again,” her words traveled the short distance
between her lips to his. They kissed and began rolling their mouths over
each others bodies and soon they began making love, again.

Moments after their ecstasy the earth bound troubles remained.

“We cannot stay here. The Blox will find me-find you and administer the
laws demanding dosage for our transgressions.”

Tony was not speaking of the actions they had taken in sleeping
together-he was speaking about the blue arcs they had shared.

“I know,” her voice was soft and hushed.

“I know of a place,” his reassuring tone made her beautiful eyes lock
onto his. “It’s a place not too far from here. We’ll need to gather only
what is precious and leave very soon.”

Her left arm burrowed between his back and the bed with her right arm
stretched out over his abdomen. She tightened her grip and hugged his
thick muscular body.

“I have all I need,” she whispered softly in the direction of his heart,
“Right here no need to pack.”

He kissed her forehead and stroked her long curly red hair. They both
inhaled a deep relaxing breath.

Roots of the Underground 

“What’s in a label? Isn’t it simply a few vowels and consonants arranged
in a certain pattern distinguishing itself from another certain
pattern?” Willie’s half crazed words somehow rang true to the concave
reflection he was speaking too in the silver spoon he was holding
directly in front of his face.

“Your label is meaningless to me…to any of the few whom inhabit this
place.

“From now on… no labels… we need no use for labels for this new
existence. Why should we bother with blacking our new commonwealth with
remnants of the past and the dying world above? What good are the spoils
from a place ravaged by nothing but failure built upon failure?

“Down here in the underground the revolution is to take root. From now
on this,” He clutched the silver spoon in his meaty fist and displayed
it to the few who were listening to his crazed words. “This is to be our
symbol. This is our very lively hood. We do not simply escape the
aboveground with the mechanics of this. We will build a new nation with
our collected fists clutching our collective spoons.”

There was little response to Willie’s impromptu speech. He had not
gilded the lily properly for a speech like that, but slowly dirty fists
clutching dirty spoons raised in salute to his.

The dirty faces in the room looked scared in reflection to Willie’s
speech. They could not grasp the wholesale sacrifice Willie was asking
of them. Their labels were the only thing they had that even the
Fatherhood refused to disregard.

Doubts and Other Thoughts

“There are only a certain amount of unknown futures,” Tony’s words
echoed in the almost empty bedroom, “This is only one of them.

The room was blanked by the sunlight pouring into it. A bed was pushed
into a corner, one set of dresser drawers were in another and a night
stand stood by the bed with a duration counter whirling mechanically.

A part of Tony’s mind clicked like the duration counter just two feet
from his head as he lay on the bed.

In his head echoed the words, “I shouldn’t have found her. Should I have
left the underground?” Tony’s words ached through him like a boxer
waking up after a night of round robin prize fighting.

“I should have at least slept some where else but beside her,” the
thought hung jerking like a rat with a noose around its neck between his
ears. The rat twitched and hung there in metaphorical agony between
TOny’s ears. Even philosophers fear to tread on the uneasy ground that
Tony awoke to that morning.

The soft purr of Personati’s breathing turned Tony’s eyes from his
glazed entrenched thoughts and down to her as she lay with her back
exposed to him. The milky whiteness glowed at Tony while he stole this
sight this morning. Tony knew the small rose chamber was not all the
underground offered. Neither the peanut butter store of Lucinda nor the
tunnels of sewage was the sum total of the experience yet to be
understood in the bowels of this strange city. Tony sat up.

“Do we need too leave soon?” her voice carried easily over her small
frame.

“Yes, we should try to get down below as soon as possible,” his voice
deep, calm and resonating.

Personati rose from under the covers pushing the off her nakedness like
an earthmoving machine stripping the sod from the dark brown soil.

“Then let us not be sluggish,” she spoke as she kissed him on his
shoulder and sprang out of the bed.

###

A large man stood over Willie’s right shoulder. Round forearms crossed
over a rotund chest. An empty dead stare glared from his eyes. The stare
was one that a gangster gives to a mark he is shaking down for
protection money—a stare he gives too a person he cares nothing for. A
stare that doesn’t warrant anything but the truth to the questions the
asker is asking. A stare that looks right through the other person-a
thousand yards past them. The person looked at is not even a piece of
meat because the person employing the stare does not want or care about
anything the person being stared at could possibly give the stare-giver.

Thick black hair curled over the meaty round forearms. A black shirt
encased the remainder of this animal’s muscular potential. He is an
animal employed because that is all he ever wanted to be and the only
reason why Willie employed him.

His label was Toro Cerberus. He was a mountain of a man covered in thick
curly black hair.

Willy was leaning forward with his hands aiding in his auditory
announcements that were ensuing as the dirty faces listened intently to
them.

“We are the underground,” Willie’s rhetoric droned. Even Toro ignored
most of the words.

###

Only the best Conspiracies

Willy stood on the empty stage admiring the vast openness of the
cavernous theatre.

This place had once been a place where people would come to hear lies
performed. They would come to see great explorations of the human
condition displayed in live action. Great fantastic sword fights with
the sound of the metal against the metal ringing throughout the
building. Amazing monologues delivered by crisp pronunciation from a
single tongue to be heard by a thousand pairs of ears. Love stories
about star-crossed lovers and terrible tragedies about kings killing
kings all for the power of the throne. Audiences heard great dialogues
between a few characters concerning the fates of entire nations. But now
the only patrons to this stage were the rats that scurried in the
darkness. The only actors and actresses were the faint ghosts of wind
that would find a way into this place.

Once known as The Skeleton, an eccentric art house at its best. Closed
by a Fatherhoods Cultural Reform. The theater defied the list of
prohibited subjects that were deemed indecent and dangerous to the
whole.

The Skeleton once held midnight showings of campy horror movies. Held
festivals for young filmmakers. There was a passionate love for this
theater felt deep within its parcel of the old District.

The Fatherhood passed a cultural reform banning theaters, “and all
frivolity that encourages decadence or dissent.”

Then the Skeleton was no more.

One morning an employee found the doors nailed and chained shut. The
owners missing from their home - condemned and escheated to the general
benefit fund; as dictated in the text of the reform as copies were
pasted over the windows.

Tearing away a bit of the paper you can see through to the lobby, the
concession stand still filled with bags of candy and popcorn - now
rotten.

a few hundred. A. Small modest stage for limited dramatization under a
