you,” Tony couldn’t be sure of things he just said but he had to
believe.

Personati leaned close to Tony’s ear and said, “What ever happens…I Love
you.”

Shocked, He had never heard those words directed towards him.

“How touching,” Toro stood in the entryway of the tunnel, “The lovers
have found the will to speak such delicate words.”

“What do you want?” Tony stood up and puffed out his chest.

“Willie wants you… Come along,” Toro tapped his outer thigh and motioned
for Tony to follow.

“No,” Tony clenched his teeth and made fists with his hands. An action
he was not used to doing and therefore it was rather alien to him.

“Willy wants you… Come along,” Toro snapped his fingers and large men
appeared out of the darkness of the tunnel behind him.

For an instant Tony thought to seize Personati in his arms and run for
the exit tunnel—to run for the safety of the aboveground. He thought
dealing with the Blox would be more civil than the likes of Toro. Them
Toro snapped his fingers a second time and two men appeared out of the
exit tunnel blocking their escape.

“Tony…Willy is waiting for you,” Toro spat on the ground and turned
towards the tunnel leading deeper into the underground: the two guards
behind Toro slid aside like the automatic doors of the aboveground and
allowed Toro to pass. The two guards, who blocked the exit to the
aboveground, stepped towards Tony with their hands outstretched. They
seized Tony by the arms and lifted him up off the ground. Tony could
smell the fresh scent of peanut butter on their breath and sweat on
their skin. Personati slapped the guards on the shoulders, but they left
her alone.

“Leave the female, Willie said nothing about her,” Toro’s words echoed
through the emptiness of the blackened tunnel.

A guard slapped Personati with the back of his hand and knocked her to
the floor where her head hit the cold brick of the wall knocking her
unconscious.

“Personati!!! Personati!!” Tony yelped as they dragged him into the
darkened tunnel.

Personati was slumped against the brick wall. She was breathing, but now
she could only be dreaming.

A few steps into the journey to the skeleton stage Toro stopped and
turned around to face his men.

“Take him to the theatre. I’ll be along shortly.”

Toro then turned down a tunnel in the direction of Lucinda’s peanut
butter store.

Toro’s men dragged Tony deeper into the darkness.

Coup

Personati’s head pounded in its skull; her foot lay in a trickle of
water leading into a drain in the center of the room. Slowly, very
slowly she pulled and built herself to standing. Still coming to her
senses while rubbing her head and neck.

Personati had to find the theatre where Toro took Tony. Her feet
splashed in the stream of water draining in the center of the tunnel as
Personati cautiously wandered deep into the underground. The blue glaze
of light that slicked the tunnel walls looked like freshly spilt blood.
Then she heard the sobbing of a female voice.

“Shut up, Bitch!” a harsh growling voice was heard from deep into a
tunnel leading to Lucinda’s store.

Then a horrible scream came from the same direction. Personati
cautiously followed the horrible sound to witness a terrible scene.

“Why are you doing this to me?” Lucinda cried while hanging by a single
thick cord of electrical wiring with a light bulb burning into her
wrists.

“Progress…Growth…Imminent Domain,” Toro said as he circled around her,
as a wolf would do a campfire at night. “Whatever the proper term should
be.”

The electrical wire was cutting into Lucinda’s flesh making thin
trickles of blood stream down her wrists as the bulb burnt the skin of
her forearms.

“You see kitty cat. You’re standing in the way of the underground making
any real headway down here. Without you in here we won’t have to keep
scrounging up gold and precious things to keep buy the PB from you.” The
cold tone of his voice sent shivers down Personati’s spine as she
listened from the darkness of the tunnel.

“What do you expect? It doesn’t make itself! You think by killing me
you’ll cut out the middleman. I am the maker. I made all of it.”

“Lies, lies and damn lies. We know it gets slipped to you from the
river. You fish it out of the river. Willie’s seen you do it.”

“That’s only the ingredients! I make…”

“Silence! You couldn’t just accept the new way of thinking around here.
The gold you horde down here with the peanut butter, it isn’t yours
anymore. Everything you once were now belongs to the underground. This
store,” He paused and looked around at the shelves of tiny glass jars of
peanut butter, “it’s now under new management.”

“Just let me go, please,” Lucinda said meekly her eyes leaking tears.

“Can’t. You haven’t learned any respect yet.”

“I won’t tell anyone about any of this… I’ll just…” she was silenced by
Toro grabbing a fist full of her hair.

“I know you won’t. Cause you’re a good kitty,” his teeth bared
themselves and his hand produced a knife from under his dirty shirt.

“How many you got left? Hmmm? Luce? Is it eight or seven? I lose count,”
his fist held the gleaming knife and pounded like a piston into
Lucinda’s abdomen. Her body contracted for the first two thrusts but
after the third she was lifeless.

Toro stabbed Lucinda nine times-four in the abdomen-four in the
chest-one to the neck.

His panting was chorused by the sound of the electric wire Lucinda’s
body swung under. The uneasy creak of the wire seemed like echoes of the
violence that had just occurred.

Personati did not dare move, the scene had been burnt into her memory.
She could barely keep herself pressed up against the cold wall of the
tunnel. She prayed that Toro would not hear her. But she could not help
the pounding in her chest and the sound of her breath. Toro’s shadow was
moving about in the remnants of Lucinda’s store. She closed her eyes as
tears streaked down her cheeks and she teased with the thought of her
running in the opposite direction but then she felt his acrid warm
breath on her face.

“Hello, little one.” He spoke.

She felt a fist impact the side of her face between her eye socket and
her ear and then nothing but the coldness of the tunnel floor.

 

Duress

“Tony! Brother!” Willie’s arms expanded widely waiting to collapse in on
Tony’s bulky frame in the action of a hug. “Today is a great day for the
underground. We are making great strides in our revolution,” a few
sheets of paper were being clutched in his left fist as Willie jovially
patted Tony’s back with his other hand. Willie released Tony from the
hug.

“I need you to speak to the underground. Here, read this for me,” Willie
thrust a thin stack of papers like an attacker thrusts a knife at his
victim.

Spit pooled in his mouth—like a clam with a bit of sand—a new sour
gumdrop was forming in Tony’s mouth.

A podium stood alone in the center of the empty stage. A single lantern
was hung to shoulder height from a rope lowered out of the high rafters
behinthe tattered main curtain of the skeleton stage.

The words scratched onto the papers defaced the whiteness of the
processed wood fibers. Black ink, a wild an abusive style of handwriting
and the words only emphasized the state of the fevered brain that
composed the fractured speech.

Tony stepped out of the darkness behind the curtain to see the theatre
filled with the dirty blank faces of the underground inhabitants.

“Brothers and Sisters, the underground is alive,” Cheers and shouts of
joy interrupted Tony’s puppetry of the words designed for him. The body
mechanics of his vocal cords and mouth movements took over as Tony’s
mind detached itself from his servitude and formulated his escape, with
Personati in tow, from this collectivist nightmare.

“Our underground is strong. Built with the total of all your efforts and
the leadership of our leader Commander Willie!” the blind applause again
interrupted his words and seemed like a wind blowing in Tony’s face. The
mass of people held in the seat less theatre seemed less than human with
every word out of Tony’s mouth. Every sentence of the collectivist
propaganda Tony was being forced to recite. After every paragraph of
Willie’s words served via Tony’s mouth they looked closer to cattle than
the human beings they were before the underground had infected them.

“We are building…” Tony paused while he rolled the sour mass of spit in
his mouth. He smiled at the tainted sheets of paper. He turned his head
to see Willie standing solemnly behind the first tattered curtain. A
grunting laugh escaped Tony’s body. A laugh that pulsed up from his
guts, shot through his chest and shook his throat as it expounded. The
drop of spit, like a piece of grit in a clam’s mouth, was spat out on to
the wooden stage floor. The crowd gasped with a stunned ignorant
response. “Nothing,” Tony spit again on the papers and then threw them
into the air above his head.

“Like every attempt at this collectivist utopia or whatever you believe
this is… You are building nothing! Nothing but a pipe dream! A broken
machine that will never produce anything but a collection of fat under
your malignant Commander Willie’s chin.” Tony grabbed the lantern,
tearing it down from the rope, and smashed it against the tattered dusty
curtain of the stage.

Arms encased Tony, a large hand cupped over Tony’s mouth as sweaty arms
and bodies dragged him off stage as the curtain dropped in a cloud of
dust and smoke.

Tony was carried away in a nest of arms and hands all holding him very
still. He could hear Willie cussing behind the steps and grunts of the
men that were carrying him. The men were taking him below the stage deep
into the dark foundations of the underground. The walls turned from the
painted grays and whites of the theatre to the dark brick and pipe of
the underground walls.

Tony noticed the faces of the men change as they stepped into the
underground. Their faces seemed to get dark and caveman like. Their
eyebrows seemed to grow heavy and their jaws thick.

Sweat began to ease off of their faces as Tony allowed his body to go
limp. Their collective of arms strained under Tony’s heavy frame. Tony
had stopped struggling against them-he didn’t care. In the back of
Tony’s mind he knew Personati was safe back at the chamber. He knew she
would get to safety, however oxymoronic it may sound, above ground. He
knew that the pigs that were dragging him had left her untouched.

Door after door opened as Tony watched from his horizontal position. He
watched the doorframes pass over him as the men struggled to carry him
below.

Tony heard heavy breathing when they entered the last room. Then he
heard the sound of a fist hitting human skin.

“Drop him there and leave us,” Willie had no emotion in his voice. The
men did as Willie commanded and left the room in singe .

Willy squatted over Tony’s head and spoke, “Now, what did you think all
that would do?” Willie paused and waited for a response, “Answer me! You
horrible bastard! You’ve set us so far back now with all your
backstabbing and plotting against me with… her,” Willie slapped the side
of Tony’s face turning his head to see evidence of what Toro had been
working on.

Personati was chained to a wall that was slowly being painted a crimson
red. She was naked, bruised and covered in filth. She could barely raise
her head to look at Tony.

Tony’s abdomen muscles clenched as he attempted the motion to spring up
and tear into action, but Willie had pulled a revolver out of his coat
and pressed it to Tony’s forehead just in time.

“Willie, a gun to someone’s head means nothing when the gun is simply
put to someone’s head,” Tony spoke without thinking.

“You know what, Tony,” Willie cocked the hammer of the pistol, “You’re
absolutely right.”

The hammer snapped forward.

Tony’s hands clenched tightly at the sides of the podium as the sound of
the gunshot reverberated in his ears. His knuckles were white with his
grip. The ball of spit in his mouth was still there, rolling from side
to side behind his molars. Tony’s head turned to the left to see Willie
standing behind the main curtain with his arms crossed over his chest
and a scowl smeared on his face. Tony’s attention back to the mass of
dirty faces waiting for his next prophetic word. At his hands lay the
stained white paper with the words defacing them.

Tony’s breath shook a little as he began reciting the speech just as
Willie had written it.

###

Toro’s footsteps echoed through the bowels of the theatre as he stepped
through a scar-like opening in the basement. Swung over Toro’s shoulder,
like the carcass of a dead deer, was Personati. She was still knocked
cold by Toro’s vulgar punches.

Toro slumped her in a corner and attached a leather collar to her neck.
Then he hooked a long silver chain to it and held tightly to the end of
it in his large sweaty fist.

The cold of the chain against her face awoke Personati. Her eyes
fluttered open to see Toro crouched over her with his arms crossed over
his knees, like a child trying to warm itself over a fire. He had a cold
lifeless stare, as his eyes cut into Personati.

“What do you expect of me? Hmmm? Little one? What would you wish me to
do?” Toro carefully brushed a few strands of Personati’s red curly hair
away from her bruised forehead. “I can’t let you run and tattle your
tail to the fools that might be foolish enough to believe you.”

His arm, that he used to brush away her hair now dangled like a phallic
symbol between his legs. He began to rock back and forth on the balls of
his feet. His muscles glistened with sweat and his face was dirty with
blood and dirt.

“You’ll have to stay here little one, till Willie sees what you have
done.”

“What have I done?” She dared to ask, “It is at your hand that Lucinda
is dead.”

“I do what needs to be done. The jobs that no one will do… I take care
of them. I see that the underground is prepared to grow and flourish…”

“And if a few people disappear—it’s nothing right?” She dared to
interrupt.

Toro pointed a finger in her face, like the barrel of a gun, “You’re
protected by my actions. She,” his arm pointed in a wild direction back
into the underground, “would have bled us dry. Usurped the energy of
this great idea. Focused us solely on providing her with gold and
valuables for the very fuel of this dream we are subject to. She was a
parasite. A useless token of the capitalist-whore mongering that ignores
the basic rights of this our new underground.”

“So you are the judge and the jury.”

“Yes, to the extent that it is I that do the jobs that need to be done.”

“If by jobs you mean killing the ones that get in the way of yours and
Willie’s dream.”

Toro stood up and began pacing in front of Personati; the silver chain
dangled between them.

“What would you do to ensure the next day?” Toro asked with anger in his
voice.

“Live.”

“That will soon be subject to change. This,” he pointed wildly around
him like a cowboy shooting off his six-shooter in a drunken rage, “is
all subject to change. The weakness of human flesh has but little real
meaning in this excursion. This is a social experiment at a pure state
where all are equaled…regardless of design, natural or unnatural. Can’t
you see that we are exploring new territory with this underground?” He
pleaded with her.

“And still making all the same mistakes that so many have made so many
times before.”

“Silence, bitch!”

“That’s it, right there. That’s exactly what all the others before you
have said and then the forced silence of gunfire.”

Toro lunged down to Personati’s eye level with his fists pressed against
the wall, her head between the two of them.

“You’ve said enough. Uncle Willie isn’t like any of those before him. I
am not the animal you paint me to be. This is not the same idea
repackaged and sold for some capitalist profit!”

A long pause of silence painfully stood between them.

“You know, you just might be right. In some facet of your ignorant
brain, you just might be right. Somewhere, sometime you might actually
be right,” her words eased Toro’s expression on his face. He leaned back
to a crouched position, back onto the heels of his feet. “Your only
problem is…this is earth…this is a place called reality.” Her words sent
Toro into a silent rage as he paced and spat on the floor. He rubbed the
silver chain in his fist between his fingers and ground his teeth.

She began again: “All your collectivist dreams will mire themselves into
a pile of exhausted refuse and the souls of all the ones you crushed
will haunt the ones that are next. You’ll reduce man to a parasite that
lives off the sweat of his brother’s brow and then the world will only
have the broken dream of your collectivism. The world will not be a
workers paradise. A place where ever man is given proper credit for his
work, but rather the worthless knowledge spouted by the propagandists
and their propaganda machines.

“Man lives for the sensation of his hands welding the strength to shape
his world. Man works to provide for his family-for the open mouths of
his children...HIS CHILDREN! You would make the entire world a ward of
the state and crush the patriarchs under the strain of providing the
entire world with food, work, shelter and water. It is impossible to ask
a man to sacrifice his blood, sweat and tears for a soul he does not
know of, care about or see with his own eyes. Ask a man to feed himself,
he’ll ask for you to show him how. Your entire system is basing itself
on the broken backs of immature dreams. A man can’t live for any other
person than himself and his children.”

Toro was fevered with sweat and a angry look upon his brow, “No…No!
No…No!” that was all he could muster in retort. He paced franticly
before her. The chain glistened in the pale light.

She began again: “You are ignoring the inherit predicament of man. It
takes something special in a man to be a leader, a poet warrior.
Therefore not all men are equal in all respects and forced equality is a
bastardized fictitious bubble of air in the pipes of rational thought.
Your underground is not promoting you utilitarian dream of equality and
overall happiness… it is crushing the people you say you are protecting
and serving. One man cannot control all the possibilities of all the
people involved and Willie is attempting to do just that!”

“No…”

“Yes, the killing of Lucinda not only displays all of this but also that
Willie will stop at nothing to get all the power for himself and ignore
the souls he has dispatched in doing so.

“Your simply his pawn. When the time comes will he not excuse you like
he has excused Lucinda? Will he not order you to take my life for all
the things I have said, will say, stand for now and symbolize in the
future? What do you expect? A grand station at Willie’s side? You’ll be
lucky to receive a scrap of what he’ll be engulfing by fleecing the
masses for his benefit!”

“No, stop!” Toro swung his boot in a swift sweeping manner catching
Personati’s jaw at the apex of the gesture. The blow sent her to the
floor. “Lies all lies! Wily is the father of all this why would a father
want to see his child fail in the manner of which you speak?”

Personati could not respond if she wanted to, her jaw was broken by
Toro’s boot and she lay unconscious.

Toro paced the room the silver chain dangled in the air attaching him to
her. Faint echoes of Willie’s speech could be heard with confirming
cheers emanating from the crowd.

“Willy will decide what to do with you. But for now I am king. And I say
you deserve justice.”

Toro exposed his knife from under his shirt. It was still stained from
its use on Lucinda. The blade gleamed in the half-opening eye of
Personati. She winced from the pain in her jaw. If she could have said
anything too change Toro’s mind it would not have accomplished much. He
looked like a wild dog about to feed on a fallen animal. Reverting back
the animal that he was, Toro planned of teaching Personati a lesson that
will scar her like the blade he held in his fist.

In Personati’s mind she cried, “No!” as the action took place her
struggles to fend of this animal were all in vain since the silver chain
choked her throat.

“This,” Toro said as he ripped open Personati’s shirt, “is man’s true
nature.” The crowd above their heads roared.

Like an old dry log of wood being thrown on a roaring fire, Toro
willingly destroyed the last scrap of his humanity.

A muffled “No” could be heard as Toro took what he wanted.

The Mad King

The pounding of Willie’s fists on the weak podium created an awkward
rhythm to his speech. Spit flew from his mouth, sweat soaked his face
and his shirt was sticking to his body. He resembled a rabid dog barking
at an imagined enemy more that a leader of a prosperous underground
revolution.

His audience, held captive by this strange entertaining sight, was
shrouded in darkness as they stared at the lantern hypnotically swinging
just above Willie’s head.

“There is nothing here of value!” he spoke emphatically, “There is
nothing here that the aboveground values. But we have created something
with the source of the peanut butter, our inspiration…our muse.

“Do we not create with every passage we uncover? Have we not pushed back
the darkness of the underground and brightened it with our progress?
Yes! Progress!” his fist pounded on the weak podium dust rising from its
cracks. “Yes, Yes, Yes! We are the creators of our futures! The new
elite that will soon invade from below upon the aboveground! If we wish
for peace we must prepare for war!”

The crowd began to cheer; this was the only interruption Willie would
allow.

“Soon the towers of glass and steel will have nothing left in them to
justify their existence, NOTHING!”

Another cheer escaped from the crowd like a hot breath of wind against
Willie’s face. He paused and looked benevolently into the eyes of the
dirty-faced mass and began again.

Tony stood behind the tattered curtain in the same spot Willie had
occupied while Tony prefaced Willie. Tony’s body rocked a little between
his heels and the balls of his feet. He felt like a puppet dangling from
a string in the closet of his manipulator. Tony crossed his arms over
his chest and inhaled the air heavy with the scent of human sweat, dust
and mildew. Only the faint aroma of peanut butter saved this experience
from being one of complete misery. The air hung in the theatre almost
refusing to move in and out of the lungs of these trespassers occupying
its space.

Their sign—their symbol—their brand was everywhere. Tony had not noticed
it until just now. A fist with a spoon clutched in it was stained across
the walls, the podium where Willie was shouting, even the curtain behind
Willie had a large red spoon welding fist painted across it.

But then Tony noticed that even the chests of a few of the people in
attendance had this mark woven into them. A cold clammy feeling embraced
Tony’s exposed skin as he realized that these poor souls actually
believed in this malignant virus-like underground.

Willy’s shirt buttons came undone one by one over the course of this
strange display. You could see the beads of sweat roll off his face and
dropping like little steel balls upon the floor. Willie’s black hair
slicked with sweat as his hand swept back the few bastard strands of
hair that dared trespass the vision of his eyes.

“Freedom! This is about freedom!” Willie’s words echoed in Tony’s mind.

Tony thought, his mind expounding at the podium in his mind:

“Freedom—for the mass of peoples that only wished for a bit of reprieve
from the pressures of the aboveground or freedom for Willie’s open mouth
to swallow all of them whole. Freedom—given by father Willie and
enforced by Toro and his goons. Freedom—held under the dogma spit out of
the open sore of power’s influence on the human condition,” Tony thought
quietly to himself.

“Freedom is to be taken. To be the fire in our bellies to be the Peanut
Butter in our mouths!” the crowd roared at Willie’s words.

“Anything of value not earned by the receiver through the sweat of their
brow or the mechanics of their muscles is ultimately destroyed by either
the ignorance of the inherit value of the object or left to the neglect
of disenfranchised inheritance upon the head undue wealth.” As Tony
thought this a twitch pulsed in Tony’s augmented leg. A slight tremor to
his right hand as well. His head turned and looked to watch it work in
rhythmic electric mechanizations.

Red streaks of light arched over the fleshy mound as one muscle twitches
in a minor isolated muscle panic. The ache came on slowly after the
pulsing began. It washed down slowly from the tiny spot where the bicep
disappears below into the anterior deltoid. The pain was like a nail
driving itself into Tony with the beat of his heart as propulsion.
Tony’s vocal cords sat paralyzed in his throat.

A bead of sweat rolled down Tony’s nose and held on there at the tip for
a brief moment-Tony’s eyes watched it carefully. Then it dropped with a
graceful arch to the floor.

“Progress! Progress! We are all subject to progress! The ever-growing
giant of our existence that WE are progressing! The underground is
progressing!” Willie slammed his fist down upon the podium already
straining under his weight. “We are to live for the progression of each
others dreams. No longer will the elder ones dictate to you or what I
should be doing in the existence that our lives!”

The crowd—the teeming mass of human flesh, like meat in the window of a
butcher shop—erupted with a blast like sound of praise washing over the
intense pain Tony was experiencing.

Tony’s eyes scanned wildly has the papers in his convulsing arm were
crunching in his hand. His free hand covered the pulsing painful muscle
and for a brief moment he wished he could simply tear it from his side.
Nobody was taking notice of Tony. Everyone’s eyes were focused solely on
the wild display of Willie and his speech. None of them could see that
this was simple propaganda served up fresh as the real mechanics of this
underground were still just under the skin and not exposed, as a true
revolution should be.

Tony’s feet stepped slowly backward into the darkness of the stage. He
sped up his movement into a run the further he retreated deep into the
underground and away from the Skeleton Stage.

Deep into the winding stairwells and tunnels of the underground. Tony
ran full speed while clutching his shoulder still pulsing with pain.

The darkness of the catacombs was reassuring to Tony as he paused from
his escape and leaned against the cold concrete wall.

Brief spots of light led Tony deeper into the abyss-like features of the
catacombs. The ache of his arm dulled itself to a hiss of bodily energy
as Tony leaned against a valve connected to an exposed pipe. His weight
rested uneasily on the wheel of the valve and with a calming sigh
escaping Tony’s lungs the wheel gave way to weight and turning
one-quarter turn.

The creak of the valve echoed throughout the darkness like a single cry
from a stuck pig. In the darkness Tony could hear the trickle of water
turn into a heavy rush. At his feet water began to pool. The room was
flooding.

“Damn fool!” a voice that was not Tony’s shot out of the darkness.
“Don’t you know not to touch things that don’t need touching!”

A gray milky white man with leathery skin stepped into the light and
reach out his old hands to turn the valve back. Tony recoiled and
tripped into the pool of water slowly draining out of the room.

“Are you one of them making all that god awful noise up there? Shouting
and stomping like mad animals?”

“Uh…I…I got,” Tony attempted to respond.

“Lost! That’s what you got. A whole heap of lost!” Tony watched the
owner of the voice as he stepped in and out of the light.

“Who…Who are you?” Tony meekly questioned.

“I should ask who are you to trespass on a mans kingdom and attempt to
flood him out of house and home!”

“I meant no harm. I got lost and I think I hurt my arm or something,”
Tony rubbed his shoulder, the pain was now gone.

“Lost? Lost? Son, you’ve been bouncing around down here like it was some
god-forsaken pinball machine! All of you up there. Invading a man’s
privacy with constant aggravating noise. All in the past few weeks with
your constant noise. It used to be quite down here. Where a man could
sit and think and be alone from all his toilers. I know, I’ve been
watching-listening to all of your racket. If you all don’t shut up and
lay low you’ll get overrun by the Blox. Just cause they haven’t come
down here before doesn’t mean they won’t.”

His white eyes and blue corneas stared at Tony like twin beams of light
focused and penetrating deeper than just the first layer of skin.

“I’m sorry. If you’ll just point me in the right direction, I’ll…I’ll be
out of your way.”

The mans hand rose with his index finger extending directly upwards then
to the left and then to the right. A cackle spat out of his open mouth
along with a string of spit.

“Pick up that box at your feet and follow me. You wouldn’t know the
right direction to go if it was a pretty girl sitting on your face.”

Tony’s eyes focused to the darkness and saw a wooden box nudging at his
foot in the draining water. With one hand he reached down and swung the
box on to his large shoulder.

The old man waddled on old legs under the weight of an old back through
the narrow ancient stone and concrete passages—deeper into the
foundations of the city itself.

“These walls were carved long before you and me were even a spark in the
pool of human potential. I’ve found markings… all sorts of markings…Tons
of numbers. There’s even two acute angles intersecting each other with
what looks like a ‘G’ stamped in between them,” his voice sounded
excited like a ten-year-old boy showing a new friend a baseball card
collection.

“Where we going?” Tony dared to interrupt with a question.

“Almost there, my boy. Almost there.”

The old man paused at an intersection of two tunnels looked left and
then right as if he could not remember they way. His wrinkled old hand
reached up and scratched his gray stringy hair. He turned to the left
and stepped into the darkness of the tunnel. Tony paused there tempted
to drop the heavy box and take his chances with the darkness of the
tunnels alone. Then he turned with the box still atop his shoulder to
follow the strange old man.

“Make way!” the old man shouted as he pushed Tony aside and backtracked
through the intersection, “Always turn right, my boy! Make way and turn
right when in doubt!”

Tony followed a few steps behind the old man. The man began to tap along
the wall like a child does with a stick against a picket fence. A steady
simple rhythm began to sound from the pipe the old man was holding in
his fist. The pipe was the drumstick and the wall as the drum.

“He stood in line with the rest/And waited for his chance/To take his
place behind the glass/And watch the ladies dance…”

The old man’s head turned to look over his shoulder at Tony with one
wild eye.

“It’s the nature of things.” The old man continued to bellow the strange
song as he stomped deeper into the underground.

“He stands looking eye to thigh/As she looks down from above/Only to be
recognized as his former love… It’s the nature of things!” he continued
to hum in the melody of the song Tony had never heard before.

The old man turned a corner and pulled open a door revealing a darkened
room.

“Down the rabbit hole, my boy. Down the rabbit hole!” he said and ducked
into the darkened room disappearing into the inky blackness.

“Come along, my boy. Come along!” the old man called to him from the
darkness.

A match hissed against the dry wall of the tunnel to reveal the familiar
silhouette of the old mans figure standing in the middle of a room with
strange pictures pasted to the walls and wooden boxes composing
makeshift furniture.

“Come in, my boy. Welcome to El Bungalow de Infidelis. You’re more than
welcome here.” He gave a bright smile and set the candle on a wooden box
at his knee.

“Put the box over there on the others and take a seat anywhere you
like.”

Tony did as he was told and sat down. Uneasiness was etched all over
Tony’s face.

He dared to break the silence with a simple question, “They call me
Tony. What about you?”

After a momentary pause the old man replied, “Don’t nobody call me much
of anything down here, but if any body ever did say much to me, they’d
call me, Mouse.” He said as he handed a small tin can of ration food to
Tony.

“Nice to meet you, Mouse. Nice little hole in the world you’ve cut
yourself down here.” stated Tony after receiving the ration of food.

“Be it ever so humble.” Mouse grinned as they both began to eat.

Chapter Thirteen

“Where is he?” Willie mumbled to himself as he paced in front of
Personati. “I know the two of you were planning on running. Where did
you plan to meet up? Where would you have gone?”

She could not of responded if she wanted to—her jaw remained motionless
and broken in her mouth. Although it did pulse with the beat of her
blood pressure Personati dared not to move the vague space between her
collarbone and cheekbone.

“What did you expect? We’d just let you walk away! That I’d just say
that’s fine…Go ahead take your leave. Tear apart half of everything this
underground stands for!” the veins in his thick red neck were pulsing
with his blood pressure.

Personati’s mind sprang with a question but it was also muted by the
fracture of her jaw. She thought, “But really what does this underground
stand for? What type of new revolution are you building here? Looks like
a vague convoluted one-mans-dream and a thousand nightmares to the
rest.”

She would have liked to hear Willie’s responses, if only she could
speak.

“Where is he?” Willie stood over her with his fists clenched tightly.

“What could you do to me?” Personati thought, “Your little muscle man
has already had his fun. What more could you inflict upon me?”

The pulsing of her jaw resumed with intense vigor along with Toro
entering the darkened torture chamber. The wounds on her body were
pulsing now as if to say, “There he is! There is the bastard who raped
us and beat us so savagely!”

A long cut across her chest, not deep enough to be fatal but deep enough
to scar, sang with intensity as she tried to cover herself with the
shredded remains of her white shirt. There was also the steady pain
burning between her legs—a ghost of violence between her thighs.

“She won’t speak. She’s not going to give up anything that would
endanger her precious Tony,” Toro spoke as he placed a yellow cigarette
between his thick lips and struck a match against the chamber wall.

“We’ll get him…We’ll get him by flushing the pipes and seeing where he
comes out,” Toro stepped to Willie’s left side and stared down at
Personati. He held out a glass bottle with some clear liquid in it for
Willie to take.

“Yeah, gonna have to clean the pipes to get him.” Willie confirmed and
took a long drink from the glass bottle.

Personati saw it in their eyes, they were not speaking of flooding the
underground, but rather they were speaking about cleaning up what Toro
had already started with her.

They turned and exited the room. Toro was the last to leave and he
paused in the doorway and turned around. Blue-gray smoke curled from his
lips and floated in the air.

“You didn’t have to make this hard. There could have been something with
me and you,” He spit on the floor and pulled the heavy wooden door shut
with one swift motion of his heavy muscular arm.

The chain—silver and spotted with filth and blood—seemed to tighten
around Personati’s neck.

Reducation Island

Graves Island (mouse talks about)

[ ] Island Hideaway and Historical Events

• The island served as a hideout for criminals.

• Bare-knuckle boxing matches were held, attracting large crowds.

• The island transitioned from a trading post to a Confederate prisoner
camp, eventually becoming city property.

Island's Multifaceted Past

• Post-war, the land saw increased use in various unconventional ways.

• It served as trade schools, a labor house, a psychiatric hospital, and
a quarantine camp.

• An amusement park was also attempted, targeting a community previously
excluded from similar venues.

Riley's Island Development and Post-War Use

• Development of a boardwalk, dance hall, and other facilities was
completed but a grand opening was prevented by city condemnation.

• A legal battle resulted in a $144,000 recovery for the developers.

• The island served as a navy barracks during World War II and later
housed Nike missile sites before closure in 1955.

Hart Island's Burial History

• The island gained notoriety due to the deaths of 31st infantry cadets
and Confederate prisoners of war from camp diseases.

• Louisa Van Slike, the first civilian buried, died of tuberculosis and
was interred in what became the city cemetery.

• Subsequent burials primarily involved orphans, widows, migrants, and
unclaimed remains from New York City morgues.

Hart Island Burial Practices: A Historical Overview

• Hart Island saw over 500,000 interments by 1958.

• Burial rates decreased significantly after the 1950s due to
advancements in medical science and record-keeping.

• The process of retrieving remains for private burial presented
significant challenges, as illustrated by the case of John Rooney in
1878.

Island Burial Site and Prisoner Labor

• Details of a burial site on an island, where bodies, including that of
John Pete, were found.

• The discovery was made through fingerprint identification, revealing a
tragic fate.

• Prisoners, often unaware of their tasks, performed the burials under
harsh conditions, facing fear and the stench of death.

Island Prison's Deterioration

• A prison was neglected after a change in ownership.

• The island's buildings suffered from storms and lack of maintenance.

• Deterioration became severe and unmanageable.

Hurricane Sandy's Coastal Devastation

• Hurricane Sandy in 2012 caused widespread flooding and damage across
the five boroughs.

• The storm severely damaged the seawall on Hard Island, resulting in
the exposure of caskets and remains.

• Significant coastal changes and the ongoing discovery of remains were
reported even years after the hurricane.

Hart Island: A Pandemic Burial Site

• Funding challenges and the COVID-19 pandemic impacted New York City's
infrastructure and burial practices.

• The city utilized Hart Island as a mass burial site for pandemic
victims.

• By October 2022, over 1000 individuals were interred in mass graves on
the island.

Island Burial Practices During Outbreak

• Limited access to the island hampered the burial process, primarily
relying on a single ferry route.

• A significant number of deceased remain unidentified and unclaimed on
the island.

• Burial procedures involved protective measures, with even laborers
using hazmat suits due to virus concerns.

Island Trench Project: Challenges and Changes

• A trench project, initially expected to last a year, filled within two
months.

• Despite an epidemic, funding increased and operational responsibility
shifted to the recreation department by late 2020.

• Inmate labor was discontinued due to an outbreak; subsequent use of
contract workers faced resistance, with many leaving after learning the
task's nature.

Hart Island Transformation: A Photographic Legacy

• A photography project on Hart Island, starting in 1991, led to a
deeper understanding of its unusual history.

• Three years of research and photography fueled a commitment to
preserving the island's significance.

• The resulting organization's efforts led to crucial reforms, including
infrastructure improvements and removal of derelict structures.

Hard Island Project: Accessibility and Restoration

• The Hard Island project aims to increase public accessibility
year-round.

• Currently, access is limited, with the City Island ferry being the
only route.

• Restoration efforts are underway for abandoned structures like the
chapel and cottage, for public use.

Hart Island's Historical Structures

• A Gothic-style chapel, built in the 1930s, is the island's main
landmark and is undergoing restoration.

• A former worker's cottage is being renovated into a visitor center.

• Several other abandoned buildings, including a hospital, prison, and
sanatorium, are closed to the public due to safety concerns.

Parisian Sewer System: An Engineering Marvel

• The Parisian sewer system is highlighted as a significant engineering
achievement.

• Its scale is emphasized by mentioning the relocation of millions and
the extensive network of tunnels.

• A suggestion to view a separate video for more detailed information is
provided.

Notes PBU.

###

“I was sick of the Pedwalls and Numb-Juice rations. The Blox ever
penetrating electronic stares constantly hovering over my head. The
cracks in the ceiling and walls of housing dwellings. Everything up
there… I was just sick of it all.

“I wanted to escape from it. To find somewhere new, but then I realized
it followed me. It followed us all down here the very second Willie
started barking orders, and like a fool I followed suit-not thinking not
saying a goddamn thing in protest.” Tony paused, “You see I wanted to
control myself. Instead of having to be at work on certain days or
sleeping at my dwelling in a bed with some stranger some directive
lottery paired me with. I was tired of it. I wanted my life to be…my
life. I was sick of the Fatherhood doing all the thinking. I wanted to
think and feel my life for myself. Is that so evil…so wrong to desire?
Simple freedom?” Tony spoke with his feet up on crates while he reclined
back against canvas covered wooden boxes.

“Sounds like a familiar story. I’ve both heard it and succumbed to it
too. Now, don’t get yourself down, my boy. It’s not evil to want
things…that’s the Fatherhood talking. They’d say something like ‘If all
of us wanted then there would be nothing left to want’ or some bullshit
like that.

“Well, I’m not buying anymore of their bullshit. That’s why I stole away
down here. Wanted to find a new place to get away from them,” he paused
to point directly upwards.

“But, I got sidetracked with that whole survival thing. You know
food-water-living. One can’t adventure on an empty stomach. Trust me I
know.

“What I’m trying to tell you, son. Is that it’s all a rat race where
ever you go and there is always gonna be something that you hate and
can’t stand but are gonna have to tolerate because life is full of
misfortunes and pain. If it wasn’t then there is no real reason for us
to be here and this is all a dream that you’re gonna wake up from
someday.”

He paused and grinned.

“But, boy when you do. Your gonna have one hell of story to tell the
misses laying there next to ya’.” He laughed and slapped his knee.

“Speaking of ‘the misses’, you got one?”

“Oh, my god! Personati! I have to go find her! She’s up there
somewhere.”

“Hold on there son. You can’t go off half-cocked.”

The faint rumble of the Pedwalls interrupted their conversation.

“Damn things. God damn social mistrust fabricated into social policy!”

The two of them paused and looked at each other-they could not be sure
which one of them had said it.

Mouse spoke first breaking the silence; “You’ll need these things if
you’re be effective with the likes of them up there.”

Mouse handed a tan bag made of canvas to Tony. It was half full of metal
round shapes and one angled mechanical device.

“The round things explode when you pull the pin out of them. The angled
thing with a handle at one end can stop whoever tries to come at you.
Just point it at them and pull the little steel thing at the curve of
it. You should have about six shots in it. But make sure the thing is
pointed at whoever you want stopped cause it’s a hell of a show.”

Mouse had a look in his eye—a look that could be seen as a father seeing
his only son going off to fight a war—a look that could be understood as
a best friend saying goodbye to a person leaving on a journey that will
change them completely from the person they once knew into the person
they have to be—a look that could be realized in a single tear cascading
down a lonely cheek where the eyes know they won’t see the face they’re
staring at…ever again.

“Remember, my boy, the quickest way to the next world is a look in the
wrong direction. Now get going. Go on!” Mouse made a shooing motion with
his hands and Tony exited the sanctuary.

The water sloshed under Tony’s footsteps as he made his way back into
the underground.

Mouse spoke softly as he saw Tony’s shape disappear into the darkness,
“The slaves can only be free if the slaves free themselves.
Enlightenment occurs to an enlightened mind. Death only comes for the
dead.”

Mouse stood in the doorway rubbing the palms of his hands together and
feeling the grit of the dirt between them.

 

Chapter Fourteen

The tunnels twisted in on themselves as Tony trudged along in the bowels
of the underground. His aim was for the heart of it all—the Skeleton
Stage, but more importantly Willie and Toro. He knew he had to stop them
and only then could Personati and him be safe.

The markings on the walls metamorphosed from etchings on the walls
scarred into the stone to toxic colors highlighting labels, affairs,
property rights and directions. Tony’s eyes scanned the walls as he
heard the rumble of the Pedwalls continue their mechanical rise.

“Hydraulic Lifting Systems” was painted in thick white letters on a
heavy steel door, which from behind rumbled the heavy muscles that
mechanized the Pedwalls every morning and night. Tony’s left knee, still
encased within a brace, sang out with a sensational ache just before a
loud pop sounded and a gentle numbness replaced the pain like a ripple
in a pond. The rumble of the machinery nudged at Tony’s ears like a
siren’s song and Tony’s hand reached for the handle of the heavy steel
door.

Oil and steam permeated this dark room. Amazed by the mechanical wonder
that was pulsing with life in front of him, Tony reached into the tan
bag and retrieved a round object. A canister was painted red with a
single word written in yellow diamond behind it. It read, “Flammable.”

The can sat neatly along side the gears and hoses of the working
machine. The round object in Tony’s hand slipped easily in through the
opening atop the red canister once the pin was pulled from it.

Tony was out of the room and down the tunnel when the fiery blast shook
the heavy steel door, bending it slightly at its rusty hinges. After the
blast, the rumbling song of the machinery was absent and the numb
quietness of the underground settled into the auditory senses. Only the
trickle of water could be considered an annoyance now that the Pedwalls
were frozen at half-mast above the underground.

Crowd shouting and the noise of people’s feet stamping on the ground
called to Tony from the Skeleton Stage above his head. His steps slowly
brought him up from deep within the underground and he entered the
Skeleton Stage.

The crowd pulsed with a sick-strange-wild-energy; they were crying for
blood.

Willy’s voice filled the theatre through the magic of a highly polished
silver microphone sitting in front of his fat face, “A traitor willing
to sell you, the underground, for the selfish desires of one. Comrade
Toro…proceed.”

Toro stepped forward from behind Willie and slowly walked towards a
crude crate supporting the weight of the victim at the far end of the
stage.

Tony asked an under-grounder next to him, “What’s going on here?”

The under-grounder responded with, “We’re cleaning the pipes. Cutting
the fat from our ranks. We’re getting rid of the ones that would try to
keep this great revolution from succeeding.”

Tony looked back at the stage quickly to see Toro kick the crate out
from under the victim. The hooded person fell down sharply and the rope
snapped their neck to break their fall.

The under-grounders erupted with cheers and celebration. Some even began
to dance at the sight of this public murder.

The body swung gently after the kicking of the legs was done. Tony’s
hand instantly reached for the curved shape of the gun in the tan bag
and eased the hammer back. His mind dared to ask the question, “Where
was Personati?” Tony stepped slowly through the raving crowd and pushed
his way closer to the stage. Tony wanted to shoot Willie in the chest.

Then he saw something that made the blood in his veins run cold and his
skin freeze atop his muscles. A few strands of curly red hair dropped
out from under the hood covering the head of the victim. The curly red
crimson hair shined in the dim light of the theatre like the sun setting
on the horizon. Tony’s eyes grew large and his blood pulsed rapidly in
his veins.

He didn’t even aim as the first shot glanced off of the microphone just
inches in front of Willie’s face. Tony stood there, as the crowd
screamed and scrambled for the exits, like a statue stands on the lawn
of a courthouse. A trail of blue smoke rose out of the barrel of the
freshly fired gun. The gun was steady and sure as the world around it
torn into chaos. Tony had the look of a man whose world was hanging at
the end of a string and swinging with the remnants of one violent jolt.

Willy stumbled backwards and ducked under the podium after brushing
against the heated metal of the lantern illuminating his figure.

A look of terror was in Toro’s eyes as his men sprang from their posts
to attack the attacker. They fought against the crowd to get to Tony.
This gave Tony ample time to aim directly for their heads.

One…Two…Three…Four shots; his aim was with machine like precision and
the bullets borne out of the barrel of the gun caught each of the guards
directly where Tony aimed them.

“One bullet left,” Tony said quietly in his mind. The crowd was almost
out of the theatre. Tony’s eyes locked onto Toro’s. Papers floated in
the air between them. Willie was ducking under the fragile podium barely
peeking out over the top of it. Tony aimed carefully at the figure
hiding shamefully behind the podium. Toro ran at full speed towards
Uncle Willie, in vain attempt to thwart Tony’s assassination attempt.

The hammer eased itself back and then suddenly forward striking the
firing pin, which in turn struck the firing cap of the bullet igniting
the powder charge and sending the bullet screaming out of the end of the
barrel in a spiraling dance of violence.

The frail podium exploded into a thousand splinters showering the stage
in a rain of wooden fragments.

The bullet struck flesh.

Blood spurted out of the thick mass and a body dropped to the floor.

A mouth spat out blood.

A deep heavy inhale ensued and a pale gray color washed over a face.

The bullet was lodged between the left lung and the heart; it had
burrowed its way deep into both of the organs.

“Wi…Willie,” and with those words Toro died.

Willy stood up slowly. His hands quivered slightly and he reached up to
wipe some blood from his face.

“Tony?” Willie’s voice carried the short distance over the bodies
between Willie and Tony.

The sound of Tony still clicking the trigger and firing mechanism of the
gun echoed in the dark theatre.

“She was going to destroy all of this! This was more important than just
one person!”

“What do you think I am doing? Just one person.” The empty gun fell to
the floor and clattered on the cold concrete.

“You’ve got your revenge. He’s the one you hated,” Willie pointed
downward at the dead body of Toro.

“No, not done yet. I knew he was just the muscle. You still need
tending,” Willie slowly stepped backwards and stumbled to the floor.
Tony stepped slowly to the side of the stage where Persoanti’s body
hung. Willie crawled quickly towards the curtains and the safety of the
darkness.

With tears in his eyes, Tony lifted the body from the deadly strain of
the rope. He removed the loop of the rope with one hand while cradling
her body in one muscle covered arm. A beaded necklace loosened from her
neck and dark colored beads rained down upon the floor. An intersecting
ancient symbol was wedged at the crease her bosom made. The hood dropped
to the floor and her red hair softly covered her face. He carefully
brushed aside her long red hair to reveal her beaten face.

Her eyes were closed and her lips where pursed shut. Her eyes were both
blackened and her left cheekbone was badly bruised. A long black mark
along with a misshaped portion of her jaw was visible, but her face was
left, in death, in relatively the same form that Tony had seen her for
the first time when they met. Tears welled in his eyes and his heart
quivered with rage as he bent his neck and lowered his head for one last
kiss.

“I’m sorry…I should have…protected you. I got lost…I got sidetracked…I
should have done something more…I should have…”Tears dropped like soft
rain on Personati’s sweet face as Tony spoke softly, “Sleep now… my
sweet Persephone.”

He laid her body gently on the hard wood of the stage and tore his shirt
from off his chest to make a pillow to place her lovely head on. He
fought back the rush of tears he felt coursing in his soul only to hear
the heavy footsteps of Willie running for the underground.

“I’ll see you soon, my sweet Persephone. I’ll see you soon.”

Tony stood slowly. His footsteps thundered across the empty stage as he
passed the remnants of the podium the cooling body of Toro stared past
Tony into the darkness of the underground as if pointing the direction
of Willie’s escape.

Tearing down the lantern with one hand, the hot metal of it sizzled
against the sweat covering the palm of Tony’s large hand. Tony looked
one last time into the cold dead eyes of Toro and dropped the lantern on
to the heap Toro’s dead body made. The clothing ignited first, slowly,
and then the smell of burning flesh permeated the empty theatre.

Tony walked towards the darkness of the underground. His stride carried
him like a wind carries the seeds of time through a valley. He paused
once to look back at Personati’s body resting quietly amidst the flames
on the stage and then he stepped into the darkness.

Chapter Fifteen

The dim light could not mask the frantic sounds of Willie’s scared
running. Dragging himself against the walls as sweat beaded on his
forehead, Willie could hear Tony singing.

“Put the dollar in the slot… and waaatchhh the laaaadieeeees!!! Dance!!!
Come on Willie! Sing with me, Brother!!!”

Willy ran through the darkness frightened and frantic at the imposing
figure chasing after him in the darkened tunnel.

“To watch the Ladies dance!!!” Tony’s thick baritone voice echoed in the
tunnel like an angry ghost tapping Willie on the shoulder.

Willy’s left foot tripped on a pipe running just three inches above the
floor of the tunnel sending him tumbling into the now empty room of
Lucinda’s store. The fall broke a bone in Willie’s foot and the fall
created a sharp pain breaking his forearm mid way. The pain was surging
through Willie but the pain was muted by the sight of Lucinda hanging in
the center of the room.

Her arms were bound at the wrist and stretched above her head against
the dimly lit bulb; her darkened body was motionless and a fly was
hovering around it.

“Broke something? You know a foot is just a foot till it’s broken,” Tony
stood in the doorway behind Willie; the tan bag was slung over one
shoulder. “Your boy, Toro, must have done that,” Tony motioned to
Lucinda’s body.

“Tony,” Willie began,” I didn’t want this. I didn’t want…”

“What the violence? You didn’t want the blood now mixing with the sludge
down here? The shit you’re sitting in? The death you sat by and
sanctioned with the employment of that bulldog back there?”

“Tony, I…was just trying to make all this a better…”

“Shut up.” Tony stepped around Willie and wrapped an arm around
Lucinda’s body to remove the body from its dishonorable position. “You
see Willie I’m not one of your type of men. I don’t see people as means
to my ends. Nor do I see them as gears in some machine that I envision.
I am no man’s means nor am I to be another man’s slave. You would have
made all of us no better than what we were above. You would have made a
place for yourself. Built on the backs of those few fools who would have
followed you. Perhaps I could have been one of those fools.”

The body slumped over his shoulder and Tony slipped the bag off and set
it on the counter. A round object with a pin attached rolled out of the
bag and settled against an open jar of peanut butter. Tony laid the body
behind the counter and turned, on his heels, to Willie.

“What did you promise us? What was it? Freedom… Choice… Progress? What
was all this for? Was it a piggy bank for you? Or could it have been
based on the inherit rights of people. Did you look to the top as a
litmus test against the acidic repercussions bubbling up down here? I
looked for release when I stepped down here. I looked for something to
break my neck away from their grip,” Tony gestured upwards. “I guess we
found it. I guess we built it and broke it all in record time. Entire
civilizations have taken centuries for its contents to finally tear
itself apart. Perhaps we as a species are getting better at it,” Tony
paused and grinned broadly at Willie. “You know something Willie; it was
a fantastic adventure. You sack of greedy puss. But you had to get
dramatic. You had to get things going your way. At least that’s what I
saw when you brought Toro in. Why did you bring Toro in? What were you
planning? Did you think at all when you sought to enslave those poor
souls as they escaped from up there? Did you think about any of it? Or
were you too concerned with the power grab in the vacuum left by those
fools above. You promised nothing and lured us down here with this,” He
held up an empty jar, “You know it was just that…Empty,” then he threw
the jar at the floor just in front of Willie.

The jar shattered across the floor; Willie didn’t dare take his eyes off
of Tony. The air was tense and the glass blinked in the dim light of the
single yellow bulb in the center of the room. Tony reached under the
counter and pulled out a dusty jar filled with a white substance.

“Remember this stuff?” Tony held up the jar for Willie to see. “Remember
how she gave me a taste and not you? She mixed it strong. So strong, you
see, I bet it could burn,” Tony picked up a match and dragged it along
the countertop igniting it with spark and sulfur. Tony’s hand was like a
mechanical arm as he slowly placed the match at the mouth of the jar of
peanut butter. The surface layer caught ablaze and a strong yellow flame
erupted out of the jar. Beads of sweat had formed on Willie’s forearms
and forehead. Tony smiled at Willie and extinguished the flaming jar
with the lid.

“Oh you can eat the stuff, but I would tackle a whole jar.”

Tony swung violently with his hand at sent the jar airborne across the
room and then smashing beautifully against the wall; the peanut butter
stuck thickly to the brick wall, it glistened as the glass fell to the
floor.

Tony reached under the counter and began to retrieve jars and jars of
the white peanut butter.

“She was hiding it from you, because she knew you wouldn’t appreciate
it,” Tony said.

He paused, with the mountain of jars formed atop the counter; his eyes
were ablaze with a wonderful idea. “You know what, Willie; some things
are better left unsaid.”

Like a baseball pitcher Tony grabbed a jar in his right hand and hurled
it against the wall just over Willie’s head. He repeated this action and
covered the walls and floor with glass and the sweet sticky napalm
peanut butter.

Sweat covered Tony’s face as he crouched down in front of Willie. He
didn’t look Willie in the eye but Willie stared like a frightened child
at Tony.

Tony’s hand clasped a long shard of glass that resembled a knife. The
glass knife looked small in Tony’s large right hand. A quick swift
motion sliced open Willie’s throat and blood began to gush from the
wound.

Words attempted to gurgle out of Willie’s mouth but only the sound of
blood and air could be understood. Tony stood up; the brace still
attached to his leg creaked in support of his weight. Tony paused and
reached down with his left hand and unlatched the two buckles that
hugged the brace to his leg. The brace fell to the floor amidst the
shards of broken glass. Willie was wallowing on the ground in blood,
glass and napalm peanut butter.

Tony brushed a jar of white peanut butter into the tan bag and slung it
onto his shoulder. Tony retrieved two jars in both his hands and turned
to leave the store.

“Goodbye, Willie,” Tony said without looking back, “Burn in hell,
buddy.”

Willie’s eyes blinked and the blood on his hands dripped to the floor.
For a moment Willie felt relieved and scanned the wreckage of the room
noticing the waste of the sweet peanut butter. Willie tore off a sleeve
of his shirt and pressed it to his torn throat, the wound was not fatal
it was symbolic.

And then, out of the darkness two jars rolled. Two jars with the lids
taken off of them and identical round objects shoved into them. A faint
sizzling sound could be heard from within the jars. The glass shattered
on the floor and the two black objects revealed themselves to Willie. It
was the last sight Willie saw; the exploding spheres of fragmenting
steel and the ignition of the napalm-peanut butter.

Chapter Sixteen

The pink door swung open as Tony squeezed out of it. He stood up
straight, stretched his back and took a deep breath of the cold acrid
air of the aboveground. The wind was freezing cold and furious. The city
seemed to ache and whimper from abuse.

A Blox hovered awkwardly - losing flight control - in the air over
Tony’s head. Sparks spit out of the back access panel. Tony looked the
Blox directly in the scanning lenses and walked away. The street was
littered with papers, broken possessions and wrecked Blox. Tony walked
in the middle of the street stepping over the inert Pedwalls, the walls
had only raised ten inches high before they were stopped.

Entire buildings were engulfed in flames, beautiful red orange and
yellow tongues licking at the structures. A man leaned against a curb.
His hands clasped around an empty bottle of Numb-Juice. His head was in
the gutter and his feet were set atop a Pedwall. He looked broken like a
fallen statue after an earthquake. Gusts of wind coursed through the
vein-like streets.

A lamppost twisted from the pressure of the wind and tumbled down into
the old brick wall of a building. Papers, wood and flames animated the
scene as old bricks crumbled off the broken building like crumbs off the
top of a stale cake. Tony’s building that he had assisted in reclaiming
was now nothing more than a cinder black and smoldering in the twilight
beam of the sun. Glass was sprinkled on the concrete and the steel of
the building stood exposed- naked as a skeleton in the sun.

Tony walked by a Blox, it beeped and blinked it lights but all its
mechanisms for flight were malfunctioning and it sat motionless on the
ground. All it could do was beep and blink-beep and blink. Tony watched
his reflection in the high polish of the black metal. The Blox chirped
like a strange bird, as sparks burst from it’s casing-the chirping
slowly toned lower and lower and then to mute.

Tony passed by the dwelling complex where Illisa and he once lived. The
broken glass in the windows and emptiness of the building told him that
she was not there and no one would be either.

The lock was pulled from the door, like a shoulder dislocated out of its
socket, out of the wood. The leather chair was gone and holes peppered
the plaster of the walls. Trash and debris littered the floor. Wind,
from a broken window, pushed through the emptiness of the room. Tony
turned slowly around and exited his former dwelling; not home, it was
never a home.

A thin piece of fabric floated in the breeze. It twisted and turned with
the force of the wind. One could hear the sound of it fluttering like
the sound a birds broken wing makes when it is trying to take flight.
The fabric pinned itself against Tony and stayed there between a leg and
the force of wind. Tony raced down and pulled this fabric off of his
leg. It had red embroidering on it, hand embroidering done in the
darkness of underground tunnels and cross-stitched at a fevered pace.
The symbol emblazoned on it was of a fist clutching a spoon. Threads
were frayed and filth matted the edges of the fabric. A strong gust of
wind swelled around Tony as he loosened his grip on the fabric and
witnessed it dance away with the push of the wind.

Glass and debris crunched under Tony’s feet as he walked on the streets.
The expanse of the desert like fields around the city stretched out into
the horizon. He could see the smoke of fires streak the sky. The tan bag
slipped from his shoulder and caught on his wrist. The jars of peanut
butter and grenades clanked. Tony could feel an object in the front
pocket of his pants. His left hand plunged in and retrieved it. His hand
pulled out a sliver spoon; it gleamed in the fading light. He gripped
the spoon in his fist and smiled at the funny symbol the spoon made when
held in the fist of a man.

The jar of the peanut butter spun on the ground as Tony plunged the
spoon deep into the thick whiteness. The texture of the concoction was
sweet in his mouth. The sunlight slowly died on the horizon as spoonful
after spoonful disappeared into Tony’s mouth. The wonderful delight
began to warm his body as the aftertaste and fuzzy sensation of the
white peanut butter absorbed into his body. Smoke billowed out of the
drains and gutters in the street. And Tony did not care.

Tony thought of Personati. He regretted his life being so long before he
knew her. Then he regretted ever regretting the path that brought him to
her.

He thought of Illisa. He regretted ever loving her, if he even could
call what he had with her, “love.”

He thought of the dirty-faced masses of people that flocked so willingly
to the promise of the underground. Those lost shells of people,
powerless and hungry for hope.

He thought of Willie smiling at him in the warehouse; before the lies.
Or where those lies too?

He remembered Mouse and his role in this grand comedy. And Tony suddenly
did not care.

Then Tony spoke aloud, “So what now?”

Tony staring up into the darkening sky, “What’s to come of this?
Smoke…always respected smoke…because it takes something to create it.
And the smell… always loved the scent that it makes.”

The spoon clattered on the ground and jar of peanut butter, three
quarters empty, glistened as it fell out of Tony’s grip and dropped to
the ground rolling to the close vicinity to the tan bag.

The faint rumble of buildings crumbling could be heard. Different shades
of black to gray and milky white—rose in the air. Dust from dead
collapsing buildings choked the streets. There was still nobody in the
street. A cloak of the color the Fatherhood wore floated in the gusting
wind like a ghost haunting the scene of its murder. Tony’s knees
buckled. His framed dropped and his back banged against an old fire
hydrant as his abdomen bowed inward and his shoulders slumped. Wind
pulsed through the streets and the fires spread. For all the action
eating at the city it was surprisingly quiet while civilization
collapsed under its own weight.

Tony did not care, anymore.

And with a whimper the world slipped into darkness.

Book Two 

Before the Fatherhood there were Kings and Doppelgangers; Gods and
Demons.

All of, by, and for, man.

Washington, 1886

1886 - United States, Washington City, Federal District of Columbia.
Often assailed with the moniker, Empire of Mud, the young capital city's
roads were barely more than ruts after the war-torn this country apart
and now force it back together with violence in abundance.

A new capital city is slowly forming itself out of the swamps of the
mid-atlantic.

Wealth.

Power.

Control.

The old money from New York and Boston flooded south, taking up
Philadelphia and Baltimore. But those holdings and influence took
decades and generations to build in those places. Washington City was
new and different.

The old world is dead. The monarchs have fallen ill with their
inbreeding. Their offspring have grown far too inept to be reliable. It
was the science men and the blood doctors that unlocked the genetics
that allowed the best and the brightest of mankind to take the lead and
replicate their visions over generations of influence.

Their faces are well hidden, the smartest and most powerful never let
their face be seen or recorded in public. They can't afford the
blasphemy and exile from the Elite.

The chosen. the leaders. the Alphas.

The intellectuals that spurred their wealth and prominence upwards to
power and command are now finding their lives shorter than they had
hoped for. The lowest and weakest of the public were the first to feel
the precedence of the fatherhood’s influence. Jobs grew out of touch for
those even with the best pedigrees. The Whole of the country was slowly
turned into a sharecropper to a broken democracy.

It was in this lull with the waxing Democracy sunning itself after the
last of the warmongering generation died from the wounds of a war forty
years ago, Here the ruling elite harnessed the power of cloning. The
late victorian age fueled with robber-baron riches of the robber barons
accelerated the darkest of curiosities inspired by the horrors of
frankenstein and blood science of Dracula, the madness of power and
success overwhelmed them and the Science and technology exploded with
such fervor that the kings of europe couldn't grasp the concept, so they
first cloned their children, then themselves. The genetic prowess of the
rich and powerful has always been far beyond our plebeian understanding.

1899, United States, New York City. 

Here we follow a man. Johan Potifex Gorgan. In this timeline, he is a
driven man. The Industrial Revolution is in full swing, and the world is
changing at a rapid pace. In the midst of all this change, one man is
determined to make a difference. And with nothing more than a gamblers
bet on exponential terms, Gorgan’s investments and bets bloomed in
stocks and oil markets and the use of a new stock market tool, the trade
option. Suddenly a fortune that if he were to demand all the paper his
wealth was written on would deprive the world of cotton for a quarter of
the year.

In this timeline, Gorgan is a wealthy industrialist who is obsessed with
technology. Not his business.

He began the path which we follow.

The one that demands the sacrifice of life.

The one that demands the solace of life spent in total devotion. To the
rule of the Fatherhood for it is the benefited child of Gorgan and his
madness. Would the heat of a steam engine and smooth rail give our
principal his drive? This man. A commoner. But one with a fire that
inflamed all around him with the desire to lead and create opportunity.
The money flowed from this youngling-god like a wild fresh water spring.

A rich man can do a lot when the world screams for him to stop. He can
listen to the world's cries and make changes to his behavior. He can use
his wealth to help those in need. He can stand up for what he believes
in, even if it means going against the grain. He can be a force for good
in the world.

What could a rich man do?

  He can donate money to charity.

  He can start his own foundation or nonprofit organization.

  He can volunteer his time to help others.

  He can use his influence to raise awareness about important issues.

  He can speak out against injustice.

  He can support policies that benefit the poor and the disadvantaged.

  He can mentor young people and help them achieve their dreams.

  He can be a role model for others and show them that it is possible to
  use wealth for good.

  Of course, not all rich people will choose to do these things. Some
  will continue to live their lives in luxury, oblivious to the
  suffering of others. But those who do choose to make a difference can
  make a real impact on the world. They can help to create a more just
  and equitable society for all.

  And battling the inner demons can be just as fraught as the real world
  ones.

He casts his fortunes around the world seeking the genius and
technologies to command one’s own humanity and soon after he was
swindled by the ugenisists, he discovers the brilliant idea of cloning.

After years spent lost in the grief of a wife lost in childbirth, Trini.
She was actually in love with him before he had made his fortune; Gorgan
now wishes for the thing even the richest man in the known world can’t
buy - time.

Gorgan believes that cloning is the key to the future. He envisions a
world where everyone can have the perfect child, free of disease and
deformity.

He also believes that cloning could be used to create the perfect
soldiers, workers, and even leaders.Morgan is not the only one who is
interested in cloning.

The ruling elite of Europe is also eager to harness the power of this
new technology. They see cloning as a way to maintain their power and
influence.

Gorgan and the ruling elite are willing to do whatever it takes to make
cloning a reality. They fund research, they build laboratories, and they
recruit the best scientists in the world.

After years of work, Gorgan and the ruling elite finally succeed in
cloning a human being. The first clone is a baby girl, and she is named
Trini.

Trini is a perfect child: healthy, intelligent, and beautiful.

Gorgan is overjoyed and believes that Trini is the key to a new world, a
world where everyone is perfect.However, not everyone is happy about
Trini's existence.

Some view Trini as a threat, a reminder of the power of the ruling
elite. They fear that Trini will be used to create a new world order, a
world where the rich and powerful rule over everyone else.

Other’s zealotry turned her into an antichrist and farmed fear out of
generations of revelations and the end of days.

Trini's existence also raises ethical questions. Is it right to clone a
human being? What are the implications of cloning for society?

These are questions that will be debated for years to come.But for now,
Trini is just a little girl. She is learning to walk and talk, and she
is exploring the world around her. She is a symbol of hope for the
future, a reminder that anything is possible.

But even in his success he found grief for the losses gained for
progress.

He did not overcome his grief.

The solution. 

A madman’s Faustian bargain ad nausuem.

Found his inspiration from roots and tubers cobbled and foraged from the
bowels of the Amazon. His knowledge of the human body and extending the
longevity of the human body was legendary. This medicine man crude the
feeble and advanced the progeny of the fruitful beyond the normal years
of a human being.

This medicine man was found by the bluebloods. The richest of the riches
are laid at his feet, but the Medicine man waves of the gould and
jewels. After yeas of gold exploring and Amazonia conquests for the El
Dorado. The Colonizers returhn enriched from the rape of lands beyond
the horizon. These men demeaned more.

Power.

Gold.

Life.

They devised the science behind the wild medicines of the aboriginal and
native civilizations to double the helix. To then create a duplicate.

And then the embryo, and quickly - all too quickly - grew doubles of
themselves. They catered to the time when they either died or at a
steady rotation for the prime versus their demise.

The Last Eleciton

June 16, 2023: Randall Scott announces his candidacy for President of
the United States on the escalator of Scott Tower in New York City.

January 13, 2024: Scott places second in the Iowa caucuses behind
Senator Marco Rubio.

February 11, 2024: Scott wins the New Hampshire primary.

March 3, 2024: On Super Tuesday, Scott wins several key primaries but
Rubio remains competitive.

April 4, 2024: Rubio suspends his campaign, and Scott becomes the
presumptive Republican nominee.

July 15-18, 2024: Scott is officially nominated as the Republican
candidate for President at the Republican National Convention in
Cleveland.

September 26, 2024: The first presidential debate is held between Scott
and Democratic nominee Hillary Clinton.

November 5, 2024: Scott defeats Clinton in the general election to
become the President-elect.

November 8, 2024: Major news networks project Scott as the winner of the
presidential election.

December 19, 2024: The Electoral College elects Scott as the next
President.

January 20, 2025: Scott is inaugurated as the 46th President of the
United States.

November 3, 2028: Scott wins re-election, defeating the Democratic
nominee.

November 5, 2028: Scott's re-election victory over his Democratic
opponent is projected on election night.

January 20, 2029: Scott is inaugurated for his second term as President.

November 8, 2032: Scott is succeeded as President by the winner of the
2032 election; it was the last election ever held before the Fatherhood
took control.

President Scott kept his promises and for a day used every executive
order he could to dismantle and destroy the last functioning democracy
in the known world.

The government was dismantled before Scott’s first 100 days. The
remaining days of his presidency would be inconsequencial. There will be
no further “President” for these people.
